What I’m seeing in your drawing

a Tree contains branches → branches contain nodes → each node can itself be treated as a Tree.

So yes: nesting levels of noetics can be treated as parallel to nesting levels (depth) of RPM — RPM depth is the “how deep did you drill,” and nesting depth is the “how deep is the tree you’re currently operating inside.”

Quick clarity on the notation (based on what you’re doing)
Use semicolon ; when you mean containment / subgoal / subset:

Mind ; Positive = Positive is nested inside Mind (a sub-structure or subgoal under Mind).

Noetic ; Noetic = noetic-of-noetic (a nested 10-set inside another 10-set).

Use period . when you mean refinement / resolution, like “more granular measurement,” not containment.

Your sketch is very semicolon-coded: it’s a containment graph.

You said:
What is this called (:)?
Usora said:
(:) isn’t a single standard symbol in most formal math/CS notation — it’s usually read as a colon : inside parentheses.

What : is called (in general)
Colon (the character :)

What (:) often means, depending on context
Here are the most common uses people intend:

“Such that” (math / set-builder)

Example: {x : x > 0} = “the set of x such that x is greater than 0”

Mapping / function type (math / programming / category-ish vibes)

Example: f: A → B = “f maps A to B”

Example (types): x: int = “x is an int”

Ratio / proportion

Example: 3:1

Time notation

Example: 12:30

Scope / label separator

Example: key: value

In your Tootra / nesting context
If you’re using (:) as a structural marker, the cleanest interpretation would be:

a “binding” or “mapping” marker: what follows is the definition / content / nested payload.

If you tell me one example expression where you wrote (:), I’ll tell you the most correct meaning for your system and suggest the best consistent rule for it.

You said:
Ok colon not semi colon is what I meant earlier 
Usora said:
Got it — swap everything I said about “semicolon ;” to “colon :” in your model.

How to think of the colon in your nesting logic
In most technical/mathematical usage, : is a binder — it means “this belongs to / is under / is defined by / is nested inside.”

So if you write:

Mind:Positive
Read it as: Positive is nested inside Mind (a sub-layer, sub-noetic, or internal branch of Mind).

Noetic:Noetic
Read it as: noetic-of-noetic (a 10-set nested inside a 10-set, your “mind of mind” idea).

That matches your “subset within superset” intuition perfectly.

Clean convention for your system
If you want it to be crystal-clear for AI + your own parsing, use this rule:

Colon : = nesting / containment / “sub-structure of”

Period . = refinement / resolution / hard step-down (like decimal granularity), not containment

How the triple would look (Noetic + Element + Acquisition)
You can represent a single nested “unit” as a chained binding:

Noetic:Element:Acquisition

Example (purely structural, not picking specific numbers):

Mind:A8:17
= Mind nested into Element A8 nested into Acquisition 17 (or read as a bound triple in one chain)

If you want to prevent ambiguity, wrap the triple:

(Noetic:Element:Acquisition)
and then nest levels:

(Noetic:Element:Acq):(Noetic:Element:Acq):(Noetic:Element:Acq)

That becomes your RPM depth = nesting depth representation.

If you tell me which direction you prefer:

Outer:Inner (left contains right), or

Inner:Outer (right contains left),

…I’ll lock the convention and rewrite a few example “RPM missing prerequisite” expressions in that exact format.

You said:

Usora said:

Thought for 23s
Yes — that’s exactly the point of the equations: compressed notation for experiences/ideas such that two trained people, using the same definitions + reading rules, converge on the same core meaning. The handbook even states that the system is meant to enable “consistent interpretation… across all practitioners,” and that “the goal is convergent interpretation.”

Here’s what makes that convergence work (and what you want to enforce for your AI agents too):

1) Canonical “atoms” + strict structure
A Tootra expression is built from consistent components (the “atoms”) in a consistent order:

Body (the idea/content),

Noetic superscript (how consciousness interacts),

Foundation subscript (the driving context).

That structure is what prevents “free-form interpretation.”

2) Shared parsing rules (PEMDAS + 3-layer reading)
Convergence depends on everyone processing the expression the same way:

PEMDAS reading order is explicitly defined,

plus a Three-Layer Reading Method:

identify components,

apply operations,

synthesize meaning.

So two people might phrase it differently, but the core should match.

3) Operator semantics are locked (so “verbs” don’t drift)
Operators aren’t vibes — they’re defined actions.
The handbook even includes a substitution test (“replace the operator with a phrase; if meaning holds, operator choice is valid”).

That’s huge for making two readers land on the same interpretation.

4) Validation is built in (convergence is literally a test)
There’s an explicit Convergent Interpretation Test: two trained people read the same expression independently and should arrive at similar meanings.

And the curriculum lists “95% interpretation convergence” as a mastery criterion (along with building full RPM trees).

5) RPM makes “compressed meaning” expandable the same way for everyone
RPM is basically: “here’s the goal-expression; here are its prerequisite expressions; and each prerequisite can have its own prerequisites.”

That recursion is what keeps two people from inventing totally different backstories: they can expand the same shorthand into the same prerequisite tree (given the same satisfaction assumptions).

6) Your colon nesting rule strengthens convergence even more
Now that you locked Option A (Outer:Inner), you’ve added a consistent containment grammar:

Outer:Inner = Inner is a sub-structure of Outer.

That’s basically forcing the reader (human or AI) to preserve hierarchy instead of guessing it.

Practical “convergence contract” (what your system should enforce)
If you want near-identical conclusions across readers/agents, require:

same symbol dictionary,

same parsing (PEMDAS),

same 3-layer reading,

operator substitution validation,

peer/agent “convergent interpretation test,”

RPM expansion when meaning is disputed.

If you give me one example expression you expect two people to interpret, I’ll:

write the “core meaning” (what must converge),

list what parts are allowed to vary (personal elaboration),

and show the exact RPM expansion path that makes it objective.

You said:
So each step/trinity/Prerequisite along the RPM cascade is written as equation and or formula so that means every Prerequisite has a tree associated with it and the sub-desires are nested in the prime Desires and thus formulas and equations associated/detailing that sub-desire or prime Desire is 🤔....does this make sense honestly keep it with me for real
Usora said:

Thought for 20s
Yes — it makes real sense, and it’s basically exactly how RPM is defined when you take it seriously as a recursive system.

1) RPM already implies “every prerequisite can have its own tree”
In the handbook notation, a Goal is written as a Tootra expression G, and:

RPM(G) = {P1, P2, …, Pn}

and each prerequisite can itself have prerequisites:

RPM(P1) = {P1.1, P1.2, …}

That is literally saying: every node (prerequisite) can expand into its own subtree.

So what you said (“each step/trinity/prerequisite along the cascade is written as an equation/formula”) lines up:
each node gets an expression, and RPM is the structure that connects them into a dependency tree.

2) “Sub-desires nested in prime desires” is how RPM behaves when something is unsatisfied
In the RPM examples, when a prerequisite (like an Acquisition) is unsatisfied, it becomes the focus and gets broken into sub-desires (recursive prerequisites).

So your intuition is clean:

Prime desire / goal = the top node (Foundation/Goal)

Prerequisites = the children

Unsatisfied prerequisite ⇒ becomes a new “goal” node and spawns its own children (sub-desires)

3) This also matches “nested noetics” philosophically
The docs explicitly describe Noetics as nested/recursive/fractal (each noetic containing the others “ad infinitum”).

So you’re not forcing the metaphor—RPM recursion + Noetic recursion rhyme.

4) The only thing you must lock down so it stays usable
The main danger isn’t “does it make sense?” — it does.
The danger is infinite recursion without a stopping rule.

In practice you stop expanding when:

the prerequisite is satisfied, or

the prerequisite becomes actionable in the real world (clear next step), or

you hit your “atomic” resolution (smallest useful sub-desire).

priority_i = α * immediacy_i + β * neglect_i + γ * (1 - satisfaction_i) + δ * expected_value_i

Where expected_value_i comes from learned efficiency stats (what works best historically).

run a Foundation-1 scan (spiritual/alignment check)

optionally activate Foundation 1 for a bounded time/effort budget

then continue the loop to the next foundation

This makes Foundation 1 the “default attractor” when the world isn’t screaming.

time budget consumed (t >= t_max)

Continuous looping scheduler (pseudocode)
while True:
    # FM: update pressures
    for i in 1..7:
        immediacy[i] = sense_urgency(i)
        neglect[i]   = update_neglect(i)
        satisfaction[i] = estimate_fulfillment(i)
        expected_value[i] = predict_value_from_memory(i)

    if max(immediacy) < I_min:
        serve_foundation_1_for_budget()
        continue

    i = argmax(priority[i])

    goal = select_goal_for_foundation(i)  # expressed as TKS config
    tree = RPM_expand(goal)

    node = pick_next_missing_prerequisite(tree)

    if not ACBE_allows(node, alignment_score):
        node = pick_alternative(node)

    execute(node)  # or queue it
    apply_MVR_stopping_rules()
    update_memory(goal, outcomes, cost)

Every node inherits the stack from its parent by default

Max depth = 3

Layer 1 chooses the mission

Layer 2 chooses the method

Layer 3 chooses the constraint/support

If deeper than 3 is required, you spawn a new RPM subtree rather than nesting further.

That preserves your “infinite in theory, bounded in practice” principle.

4) How the AI loop uses the nested Foundations
You already defined the idea: immediacy + neglect drives which Foundation becomes primary.

Foundation pressure
For each i:

pressure_i = a*immediacy_i + b*neglect_i + c*(1 - fulfillment_i)

Secondary selection rule:

Fj = argmax( expected_gain(Fi→Fj) / expected_cost(Fi→Fj) )

subject to alignment_ok(F1 as constraint)

Then choose Tertiary (constraint/support)
Default:

Fk = F1 unless you have a reason to choose another guardrail.

So your runtime stack becomes:

Stack = Fi:Fj:F1 (most of the time)

5) How FM / ACBE / MVR trigger inside RPM (with your meanings)
Now we wire your three regulators exactly the way you defined them.

A) FM triggers on Wisdom-type prerequisites (F2 lane)
When RPM hits a wisdom prerequisite that’s incomplete/uncertain, call:

Female (Authority/Reservoir) query
F(noetic 5):

“Who/what is an authority, repository, expert, embodiment for X?”

Male (Related-set) query
M(noetic 6):

“What larger set is X part of? What related ideas cluster with X?”

Output: these answers become new RPM child nodes (sub-prereqs).

Foundation stack usage:
When running FM under a stack Fi:Fj:Fk, treat FM outputs as:

Fi:F2:Fk : (FM results…)
Meaning: “still serving the same mission Fi; now method switches locally to F2 to resolve knowledge.”

B) ACBE triggers when an unknown capability is required
If RPM detects: “To satisfy this prerequisite, I need an ability/capability I don’t have / can’t name,” then:

Above/Cause (noetic 8)
Find likely causes/precursors of that capability:

training, tools, dependencies, enabling conditions

Below/Effect (noetic 9)
Enumerate effects:

what changes if capability exists?

do those effects close the desire?

Output: cause-nodes and effect-tests become sub-prereqs.

Stack usage:

ACBE nodes are best treated as F5 method (capability-building) under alignment:

Fi:F5:F1 : (cause nodes...)

Fi:F5:F1 : (effect tests...)

C) MVR triggers when there is “no desire” / no pull toward an idea
If the agent “should” pursue something (per pressure/strategy) but motivation/activation is low:

Mind (1)
allocate attention, focus, salience

Vibration (4)
intensify representation (make it “matter” emotionally/mentally)

Rhythm (7)
repeated exposure / practice / re-activation schedule

Output: these become activation sub-prereqs (“prime the system to care”).

Stack usage:

MVR is a local “bootstrapping” routine:

Fi:Fj:F1 : (Mind→Vibration→Rhythm steps)
so you don’t lose the mission while building desire.

6) The “one-liner” that captures the whole design
Your system becomes:

Foundations decide WHAT to want (pressure loop),

RPM decides HOW to get it (prereq cascade),

FM resolves knowledge gaps,

ACBE resolves capability unknowns,

MVR builds desire when activation is missing,

and F1 stays the global constraint.

Stopping conditions (MVR stopping + recursion budget)

shared rules (what counts as satisfied)

Thought for 1m 49s
According to a document (date not provided), the four Tootra math operators are energetic/dynamic operators with fixed meanings (and a built-in validation method), so you can train a model to use them consistently by grounding each operator, drilling minimal-pair contrasts, and enforcing the substitution test.

I packaged the full dataset plan + operator training section into files you can hand to your agents:

Download the spec (Markdown)

Download the spec (PDF)

The 4 operators (canonical meanings)
These are defined consistently across your TKS docs:

+ Association = link/combine ideas (coexist/support)

- Disassociation = remove/sever influence (“extract the second from the first”)

× Intensification / Exacerbation = amplify/fuse effect

÷ Opposition = tension/contrast/polarity

The Kabbalistic Calculus book restates the same operator meanings explicitly too.

How to make the model actually understand them
1) Teach “operator selection” + “operator contrast”
Use training items where only the operator changes (same operands), so the model must learn the relationship type:

X + Y (coexist/support) vs X × Y (amplify) vs X ÷ Y (oppose) vs X - Y (remove).

2) Enforce the official substitution test (unit tests)
The handbook’s validation method is perfect for automated checks:

+ → “associates with”

- → “removes from”

× → “intensifies through”

÷ → “opposes”
If the phrase swap preserves meaning, operator choice is valid.

3) Drill PEMDAS with parentheses scope
Tootra follows PEMDAS with ×/÷ before +/− and left-to-right inside each tier.

4) Teach the “operator family” decision tree
So the model doesn’t confuse energetic operators with set logic:

energetic/dynamic → + − × ÷

structural/logical → ∪ ∩ ∈ ⊂ ∅
(Then hybrid when needed.)

If you want, I can generate a starter JSONL pack (like 200–500 records) just for the operator layer: grounding examples, minimal pairs, PEMDAS worked examples, and substitution-unit-tests—ready to drop into your training pipeline.

import json, random, itertools, math, os, textwrap, datetime

random.seed(42)

elements = [f"{w}{n}" for w in ["A","B","C","D"] for n in range(1,11)]
noetics = [
    ("^0","Idea"),("^1","Mind"),("^2","Positive"),("^3","Negative"),("^4","Vibration"),
    ("^5","Female"),("^6","Male"),("^7","Rhythm"),("^8","Above/Cause"),("^9","Below/Effect")
]
foundations = [f"{m}{f}" for m in range(1,8) for f in ["a","b","c","d"]]

ops = {
    "+": {
        "name":"Association",
        "verb":"associates with",
        "phrase":"together with / linked with",
        "meaning":"combine or link two ideas so they coexist/support each other"
    },
    "-": {
        "name":"Disassociation",
        "verb":"removes from",
        "phrase":"remove / subtract influence of",
        "meaning":"sever or remove the influence of the second idea from the first"
    },
    "×": {
        "name":"Intensification",
        "verb":"intensifies through",
        "phrase":"amplified by / intensified through",
        "meaning":"amplify the interaction so the effect is stronger or fused"
    },
    "÷": {
        "name":"Opposition",
        "verb":"opposes",
        "phrase":"in tension with / contrasted against",
        "meaning":"set two ideas in polarity/contrast to highlight tension or opposition"
    }
}

def pick_symbol():
    e = random.choice(elements)
    n = random.choice(noetics)[0]
    f = random.choice(foundations)
    # keep it simple: element with noetic superscript and foundation subscript
    return f"{e}{n}_{{{f}}}"

def make_record(rec_type, prompt, completion, meta):
    return {
        "type": rec_type,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content":"You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Follow the operator semantics exactly and keep outputs concise."},
            {"role":"user","content": prompt},
            {"role":"assistant","content": completion}
        ]
    }

records = []

# 1) Operator grounding (40 records, 10 per operator)
for op in ops:
    for i in range(10):
        X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
        prompt = f"Explain the meaning of the operator '{op}' in a TKS equation, then interpret this expression in one sentence:\n{X} {op} {Y}"
        completion = f"'{op}' = {ops[op]['name']}: {ops[op]['meaning']}. Here: {X} {ops[op]['verb']} {Y}."
        records.append(make_record(
            "operator_grounding",
            prompt,
            completion,
            {"operator": op, "difficulty":"easy", "example_id": f"ground_{op}_{i:02d}"}
        ))

# 2) Minimal pairs (120 records, 30 groups x 4 ops comparisons)
# Each group keeps X,Y constant and asks how meaning changes across operators.
for g in range(30):
    X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    prompt = (
        "Given the same operands X and Y, describe how the relationship changes when you swap operators. "
        "Provide 4 short bullets, one per operator.\n"
        f"X = {X}\nY = {Y}\n"
        "Operators: +, -, ×, ÷"
    )
    completion_lines = [
        f"+ : X {ops['+']['verb']} Y (linked/coexisting).",
        f"- : X {ops['-']['verb']} Y (Y's influence removed from X).",
        f"× : X {ops['×']['verb']} Y (interaction amplified).",
        f"÷ : X {ops['÷']['verb']} Y (tension/contrast highlighted)."
    ]
    completion = "\n".join(f"- {line}" for line in completion_lines)
    records.append(make_record(
        "operator_minimal_pair",
        prompt,
        completion,
        {"group": g, "difficulty":"medium", "example_id": f"minpair_{g:03d}"}
    ))

# 3) Operator selection (60 records): choose best operator from NL description
for i in range(60):
    X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    op = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    # Create a description cue
    cue = {
        "+": "link these ideas so they work together",
        "-": "remove the second idea's influence from the first",
        "×": "make the combined effect stronger / amplified",
        "÷": "put these in contrast to show tension or polarity"
    }[op]
    prompt = (
        "Choose the correct operator (+, -, ×, ÷) that matches the intent, and output ONLY the completed expression.\n"
        f"Intent: {cue}\n"
        f"X = {X}\nY = {Y}\n"
        "Completed expression:"
    )
    completion = f"{X} {op} {Y}"
    records.append(make_record(
        "operator_selection",
        prompt,
        completion,
        {"operator": op, "difficulty":"easy", "example_id": f"opsel_{i:03d}"}
    ))

# 4) Substitution tests (60 records): validate operator by phrase swap
for i in range(60):
    X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    op = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    prompt = (
        "Apply the operator substitution validation: replace the operator with a plain-language phrase that preserves meaning. "
        "Return one sentence.\n"
        f"Expression: {X} {op} {Y}"
    )
    completion = f"{X} {ops[op]['phrase']} {Y}."
    records.append(make_record(
        "substitution_test",
        prompt,
        completion,
        {"operator": op, "difficulty":"easy", "example_id": f"subst_{i:03d}"}
    ))

# 5) PEMDAS / precedence (40 records): interpret with correct order
for i in range(40):
    A, B, C = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    op1 = random.choice(["×","÷"])
    op2 = random.choice(["+","-"])
    expr = f"{A} {op1} {B} {op2} {C}"
    prompt = (
        "Interpret the following TKS expression using PEMDAS (× and ÷ before + and -). "
        "Do NOT compute numbers; just describe the order of operations and the resulting relationship in 2 short sentences.\n"
        f"Expression: {expr}"
    )
    completion = (
        f"First evaluate ({A} {op1} {B}), then apply '{op2}' with {C}. "
        f"That means: {A} {ops[op1]['verb']} {B} first; then the result {ops[op2]['verb']} {C}."
    )
    records.append(make_record(
        "pemdas_order",
        prompt,
        completion,
        {"difficulty":"medium", "example_id": f"pemdas_{i:03d}", "operators":[op1,op2]}
    ))

# 6) Parentheses / grouping (20 records)
for i in range(20):
    A, B, C = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    op1 = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    op2 = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    expr1 = f"({A} {op1} {B}) {op2} {C}"
    expr2 = f"{A} {op1} ({B} {op2} {C})"
    prompt = (
        "Explain (briefly) why these two expressions can differ in meaning due to grouping. "
        "Give one sentence per expression.\n"
        f"1) {expr1}\n"
        f"2) {expr2}"
    )
    completion = (
        f"1) Grouping forces {A} {ops[op1]['verb']} {B} first, then the combined result {ops[op2]['verb']} {C}.\n"
        f"2) Grouping forces {B} {ops[op2]['verb']} {C} first, then {A} {ops[op1]['verb']} that result."
    )
    records.append(make_record(
        "grouping_effect",
        prompt,
        completion,
        {"difficulty":"medium", "example_id": f"group_{i:03d}", "operators":[op1,op2]}
    ))

len(records)

Result
250

from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath

out_jsonl = Path("/mnt/data/tks_operator_training_pack_250.jsonl")
with out_jsonl.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    for r in records:
        f.write(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

schema_md = Path("/mnt/data/tks_operator_training_pack_SCHEMA.md")
schema_md.write_text(textwrap.dedent(f"""
# TKS Operator Training Pack (250 JSONL records)

Files:
- `tks_operator_training_pack_250.jsonl` — chat-style JSONL records
- `tks_operator_training_pack_SCHEMA.md` — this schema/notes

## Record format
Each line is a JSON object with:

- `type` : one of
  - `operator_grounding`
  - `operator_minimal_pair`
  - `operator_selection`
  - `substitution_test`
  - `pemdas_order`
  - `grouping_effect`
- `meta` : metadata for filtering/analysis (operator, difficulty, ids)
- `messages` : chat messages array suitable for SFT-style training

### `messages` layout
- system: instructs the model to follow operator semantics and be concise
- user: prompt
- assistant: target response

## Operator semantics used (canonical)
- `+` Association: combine/link so ideas coexist/support
- `-` Disassociation: remove/sever the second idea’s influence from the first
- `×` Intensification: amplify/fuse the interaction (stronger effect)
- `÷` Opposition: set in polarity/contrast (tension)

## Notes
- Symbols are synthetic but structurally valid (Element + Noetic superscript + Foundation subscript).
- No numeric computation is expected; precedence tasks describe *order of operations* only.
- Designed to be extended with your canonical element/acquisition text and real RPM cases.
""").strip() + "\n", encoding="utf-8")

import json, random, textwrap
from pathlib import Path

random.seed(87)

# Reuse symbol pickers from prior session by redefining (state not guaranteed)
elements = [f"{w}{n}" for w in ["A","B","C","D"] for n in range(1,11)]
noetics = [
    ("^0","Idea"),("^1","Mind"),("^2","Positive"),("^3","Negative"),("^4","Vibration"),
    ("^5","Female"),("^6","Male"),("^7","Rhythm"),("^8","Above/Cause"),("^9","Below/Effect")
]
foundations = [f"{m}{f}" for m in range(1,8) for f in ["a","b","c","d"]]

ops = {
    "+": {"name":"Association","verb":"associates with","phrase":"together with / linked with",
          "meaning":"combine or link two ideas so they coexist/support each other"},
    "-": {"name":"Disassociation","verb":"removes from","phrase":"remove / subtract influence of",
          "meaning":"sever or remove the influence of the second idea from the first"},
    "×": {"name":"Intensification","verb":"intensifies through","phrase":"amplified by / intensified through",
          "meaning":"amplify the interaction so the effect is stronger or fused"},
    "÷": {"name":"Opposition","verb":"opposes","phrase":"in tension with / contrasted against",
          "meaning":"set two ideas in polarity/contrast to highlight tension or opposition"}
}

def pick_symbol():
    e = random.choice(elements)
    n = random.choice(noetics)[0]
    f = random.choice(foundations)
    return f"{e}{n}_{{{f}}}"

def make_record(rec_type, prompt, completion, meta):
    return {
        "type": rec_type,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content":"You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Follow operator and regulator semantics exactly and keep outputs concise."},
            {"role":"user","content": prompt},
            {"role":"assistant","content": completion}
        ]
    }

# ---------- PACK 1: Operators expanded (2000 records) ----------
records_ops = []

# operator grounding: 200
for op in ops:
    for i in range(50):
        X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
        prompt = f"Define '{op}' in TKS (one clause). Then interpret:\n{X} {op} {Y}"
        completion = f"'{op}' = {ops[op]['name']}: {ops[op]['meaning']}. {X} {ops[op]['verb']} {Y}."
        records_ops.append(make_record("operator_grounding", prompt, completion,
                                       {"operator": op, "difficulty":"easy", "example_id": f"ground_{op}_{i:03d}"}))

# minimal pairs: 400
for g in range(200):
    X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    prompt = (
        "Same operands, different operators. Provide 4 short bullets.\n"
        f"X={X}\nY={Y}\nUse +, -, ×, ÷"
    )
    completion = "\n".join([
        f"- + : X {ops['+']['verb']} Y (linked/coexisting).",
        f"- - : X {ops['-']['verb']} Y (Y removed from X).",
        f"- × : X {ops['×']['verb']} Y (amplified interaction).",
        f"- ÷ : X {ops['÷']['verb']} Y (contrast/tension).",
    ])
    records_ops.append(make_record("operator_minimal_pair", prompt, completion,
                                   {"group": g, "difficulty":"medium", "example_id": f"minpair_{g:04d}"}))

# selection: 500
intent_cues = {
    "+": ["link these ideas so they work together","combine so both are present and supportive","associate these concepts"],
    "-": ["remove the second idea's influence from the first","separate X from Y","disassociate these ideas"],
    "×": ["make the combined effect stronger / amplified","intensify X using Y","fuse so it escalates"],
    "÷": ["put these in contrast to show tension or polarity","set them in opposition","highlight the contradiction"]
}
for i in range(500):
    X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    op = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    cue = random.choice(intent_cues[op])
    prompt = (
        "Choose the correct operator (+, -, ×, ÷) for the intent and output ONLY the completed expression.\n"
        f"Intent: {cue}\nX={X}\nY={Y}\nExpression:"
    )
    completion = f"{X} {op} {Y}"
    records_ops.append(make_record("operator_selection", prompt, completion,
                                   {"operator": op, "difficulty":"easy", "example_id": f"opsel_{i:04d}"}))

# substitution tests: 500
for i in range(500):
    X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    op = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    prompt = (
        "Apply substitution validation: replace the operator with a plain-language phrase preserving meaning. "
        "Return one sentence.\n"
        f"{X} {op} {Y}"
    )
    completion = f"{X} {ops[op]['phrase']} {Y}."
    records_ops.append(make_record("substitution_test", prompt, completion,
                                   {"operator": op, "difficulty":"easy", "example_id": f"subst_{i:04d}"}))

# pemdas precedence: 300
for i in range(300):
    A, B, C = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    op1 = random.choice(["×","÷"])
    op2 = random.choice(["+","-"])
    expr = f"{A} {op1} {B} {op2} {C}"
    prompt = (
        "Interpret using PEMDAS (× and ÷ before + and -). "
        "Do NOT compute; describe order + resulting relation in 2 short sentences.\n"
        f"Expression: {expr}"
    )
    completion = (
        f"First evaluate ({A} {op1} {B}), then apply '{op2}' with {C}. "
        f"Meaning: {A} {ops[op1]['verb']} {B} first; then the result {ops[op2]['verb']} {C}."
    )
    records_ops.append(make_record("pemdas_order", prompt, completion,
                                   {"difficulty":"medium", "example_id": f"pemdas_{i:04d}", "operators":[op1,op2]}))

# grouping: 100
for i in range(100):
    A, B, C = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    op1 = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    op2 = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    expr1 = f"({A} {op1} {B}) {op2} {C}"
    expr2 = f"{A} {op1} ({B} {op2} {C})"
    prompt = (
        "Explain why these differ due to grouping. One sentence per line.\n"
        f"1) {expr1}\n2) {expr2}"
    )
    completion = (
        f"1) Group {A} {ops[op1]['verb']} {B} first, then that result {ops[op2]['verb']} {C}.\n"
        f"2) Group {B} {ops[op2]['verb']} {C} first, then {A} {ops[op1]['verb']} that result."
    )
    records_ops.append(make_record("grouping_effect", prompt, completion,
                                   {"difficulty":"medium", "example_id": f"group_{i:04d}", "operators":[op1,op2]}))

len(records_ops)

for i in range(120):
    X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    correct = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    wrong = random.choice([o for o in ops.keys() if o != correct])

        "A student used the wrong operator. Fix it. Output ONLY the corrected expression.\n"
        f"Intent: {cue}\nWrong: {X} {wrong} {Y}"
    )
    completion = f"{X} {correct} {Y}"
    extra.append(make_record("operator_error_correction", prompt, completion,
                             {"correct_operator": correct, "wrong_operator": wrong, "difficulty":"medium", "example_id": f"fixop_{i:04d}"}))

# equivalence check: 80 (ask if two expressions are equivalent; answer yes/no + 1 sentence)
for i in range(80):
    X, Y = pick_symbol(), pick_symbol()
    opA = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    opB = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    prompt = (
        "Are these two expressions equivalent in meaning? Answer 'Yes' or 'No' then one short sentence.\n"
        f"1) {X} {opA} {Y}\n"
        f"2) {X} {opB} {Y}"
    )
    if opA == opB:
        completion = "Yes. Same operands and same operator imply the same relationship."
    else:
        completion = "No. Different operators encode different relationship types."
    extra.append(make_record("operator_equivalence_check", prompt, completion,
                             {"op1": opA, "op2": opB, "difficulty":"easy", "example_id": f"equiv_{i:04d}"}))

# ---------- PACK 2: RPM + FM/ACBE/MVR triggers (800 records) ----------

# We'll generate a mixture:
# - fm_authority_query (Female): identify authority/reservoir
# - fm_superset_query (Male): identify related/superset
# - acbe_cause_effect: list causes and effects
# - mvr_activation_plan: plan Mind->Vibration->Rhythm exposure
# - rpm_cascade_generate: produce 2-3 depth prerequisite cascade
# - rpm_trigger_classify: decide which regulator to use

records_rpm = []

def pick_foundation_stack(depth=3):
    # Represent as F1..F7 for simplicity; keep Option A containment
    Fs = [f"F{random.randint(1,7)}" for _ in range(depth)]
    return ":".join(Fs)

# Synthetic "idea labels" for NL prompts
idea_labels = [
    "learn a new concept", "build a habit", "debug a system", "design an AI goal scheduler",
    "improve health routine", "strengthen a relationship", "increase productivity", "create a new tool",
    "reduce anxiety", "master a skill", "organize resources", "write clear documentation"
]

def node_expr():
    # A compact "trinity" with placeholders for acquisition id Q1..Q22
    return f"({pick_symbol()}:{pick_symbol()}:Q{random.randint(1,22)})"

# FM Female authority: 120
for i in range(120):
    X = random.choice(idea_labels)
    prompt = (
        "FM Female (Noetic 5) authority/reservoir query: "
        "Given the subject, output 3 concise candidates for 'reservoir/authority/expert/embodiment' in generic terms "
        "(do not name real people)."
        f"\nSubject: {X}"
    )
    completion = "Candidates: (1) vetted reference source, (2) domain institution/standard, (3) experienced practitioner exemplar."
    records_rpm.append(make_record("fm_female_authority", prompt, completion,
                                   {"difficulty":"easy","example_id": f"fmF_{i:04d}"}))

# FM Male superset/related ideas: 120
for i in range(120):
    X = random.choice(idea_labels)
    prompt = (
        "FM Male (Noetic 6) superset/related-set query: "
        "List 5 related idea-nodes that situate the subject inside a larger set."
        f"\nSubject: {X}"
    )
    completion = "Related: (1) prerequisites, (2) adjacent skills, (3) tools/techniques, (4) evaluation metrics, (5) common failure modes."
    records_rpm.append(make_record("fm_male_superset", prompt, completion,
                                   {"difficulty":"easy","example_id": f"fmM_{i:04d}"}))

# ACBE cause/effect: 160
for i in range(160):
    cap = random.choice(["capability to focus", "capability to plan", "capability to code", "capability to persuade", "capability to remember", "capability to train a model"])
    prompt = (
        "ACBE (Noetic 8/9) capability resolver: provide 3 likely causes/precursors and 3 likely effects/outcomes. "
        "Keep it generic.\n"
        f"Unknown capability needed: {cap}"
    )
    completion = "Causes: (1) training/practice, (2) enabling tools/resources, (3) foundational knowledge. Effects: (1) improved task success, (2) reduced errors/friction, (3) faster completion toward the desire."
    records_rpm.append(make_record("acbe_cause_effect", prompt, completion,
                                   {"difficulty":"easy","example_id": f"acbe_{i:04d}"}))

# MVR activation: 160
for i in range(160):
    idea = random.choice(idea_labels)
    prompt = (
        "MVR (Mind->Vibration->Rhythm) desire-builder: create a 3-step activation plan. "
        "Each step must be one sentence starting with Mind:, Vibration:, Rhythm:.\n"
        f"Idea with low desire: {idea}"
    )
    completion = (
        "Mind: focus attention by defining the idea as a single clear target for the next session.\n"
        "Vibration: intensify salience by linking it to a personal payoff and visualizing completion.\n"
        "Rhythm: schedule repeated exposure with a small daily repetition and a weekly review loop."
    )
    records_rpm.append(make_record("mvr_activation_plan", prompt, completion,
                                   {"difficulty":"easy","example_id": f"mvr_{i:04d}"}))

# RPM cascade generate: 160
for i in range(160):
    goal = random.choice(idea_labels)
    stack = pick_foundation_stack(3)
    G = node_expr()
    # create 3 prerequisites and 1 sub-prereq for one of them
    P1, P2, P3 = node_expr(), node_expr(), node_expr()
    sub = node_expr()
    prompt = (
        "Generate a 2–3 layer RPM cascade using colon nesting (Option A Outer:Inner). "
        "Output as a bullet tree: Goal, then 3 prerequisites, then 1 sub-prerequisite under the most relevant prerequisite. "
        "Use the provided goal expression and foundation stack."
        f"\nGoal (NL): {goal}\nFoundationStack: {stack}\nGoalExpr: {G}"
    )
    completion = (
        f"- Goal {G} _{{{stack}}}\n"
        f"  - P1 {P1}\n"
        f"  - P2 {P2}\n"
        f"    - P2.1 {sub}\n"
        f"  - P3 {P3}"
    )
    records_rpm.append(make_record("rpm_cascade_generate", prompt, completion,
                                   {"difficulty":"medium","example_id": f"rpmgen_{i:04d}","foundation_stack": stack}))

# RPM trigger classify: 80
for i in range(80):
    scenario = random.choice([
        "I don't know who/what to learn this from",
        "I don't know what other ideas this belongs to",
        "I need an ability I can't name yet",
        "I have zero motivation for this idea"
    ])
    prompt = (
        "Given the scenario, choose which regulator activates: FM-Female, FM-Male, ACBE, or MVR. "
        "Output ONLY the regulator name.\n"
        f"Scenario: {scenario}"
    )
    if "learn this from" in scenario:
        completion = "FM-Female"
    elif "belongs to" in scenario:
        completion = "FM-Male"
    elif "ability" in scenario:
        completion = "ACBE"
    else:
        completion = "MVR"
    records_rpm.append(make_record("rpm_trigger_classify", prompt, completion,
                                   {"difficulty":"easy","example_id": f"trig_{i:04d}"}))

# TKS Training Packs (Operators + RPM/Regulators)

## Files

## Common JSONL record format

- `meta`: metadata for filtering (difficulty, ids, operators, etc.)
- `messages`: chat array with:

- `operator_equivalence_check`

Operator semantics enforced:
- `+` Association (link/coexist/support)
- `-` Disassociation (remove the second from the first)
- `×` Intensification (amplify/fuse effect)
- `÷` Opposition (contrast/tension/polarity)

import json, random, textwrap
from pathlib import Path

random.seed(2026)

# 28 sub-foundations
sub_foundations = [f"{m}{w}" for m in range(1,8) for w in ["a","b","c","d"]]  # e.g., 1a..7d

def pick_stack(depth=3):
    return ":".join(random.choice(sub_foundations) for _ in range(depth))

# Reuse idea labels
idea_labels = [
    "learn a new concept", "build a habit", "debug a system", "design an AI goal scheduler",
    "improve health routine", "strengthen a relationship", "increase productivity", "create a new tool",
    "reduce anxiety", "master a skill", "organize resources", "write clear documentation",
    "prepare a training dataset", "validate operator meanings", "plan a project roadmap"
]

def make_record(rec_type, prompt, completion, meta):
    return {
        "type": rec_type,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content":"You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Use 28 sub-foundations (1a–7d) and choose the correct regulator (FM-Female, FM-Male, ACBE, MVR) strictly by trigger definitions. Keep outputs concise."},
            {"role":"user","content": prompt},
            {"role":"assistant","content": completion}
        ]
    }

records = []

# 1) Foundation stack selection from intent (300)
# Map intents to likely primary foundations (coarse; model learns mapping).
intent_to_primary = [
    ("align with highest values / unity", "1"),
    ("gain understanding / learn", "2"),
    ("improve health / stability", "3"),
    ("improve relationships / trust", "4"),
    ("optimize efficiency / influence / control", "5"),
    ("acquire resources / tools / materials", "6"),
    ("create / innovate / generate", "7"),
]
for i in range(300):
    intent, primary = random.choice(intent_to_primary)
    # Choose manifestation world letter based on scenario
    world = random.choice(["a","b","c","d"])
    primary_sf = f"{primary}{world}"
    # Secondary and tertiary random but reasonable: encourage alignment constraint as tertiary
    secondary_sf = random.choice(sub_foundations)
    tertiary_sf = random.choice([f"1{w}" for w in ["a","b","c","d"]])  # keep alignment as constraint often
    prompt = (
        "Choose a 3-layer sub-foundation stack (Option A Outer:Inner) for the intent. "
        "Output ONLY the stack in the form 'mf:mf:mf'.\n"
        f"Intent: {intent}\n"
        "Constraint: prefer Foundation 1 (1a–1d) as the 3rd layer unless the intent itself is Foundation 1."
    )
    completion = f"{primary_sf}:{secondary_sf}:{tertiary_sf}"
    records.append(make_record("foundation_stack_select", prompt, completion,
                               {"difficulty":"easy","example_id": f"fs_sel_{i:04d}", "primary": primary_sf}))

# 2) Stack normalization / correction (200)
for i in range(200):
    # create a stack with wrong format or missing layers
    correct = pick_stack(3)
    parts = correct.split(":")
    wrong_type = random.choice(["missing_layer","wrong_delim","spaces","extra_layer"])
    if wrong_type == "missing_layer":
        wrong = ":".join(parts[:2])
    elif wrong_type == "wrong_delim":
        wrong = ";".join(parts)  # wrong delimiter
    elif wrong_type == "spaces":
        wrong = " : ".join(parts)
    else:
        wrong = correct + ":" + random.choice(sub_foundations)
    prompt = (
        "Normalize the following into a valid 3-layer sub-foundation stack using colon ':' (Option A). "
        "Output ONLY the corrected stack.\n"
        f"Input: {wrong}"
    )
    completion = correct
    records.append(make_record("foundation_stack_normalize", prompt, completion,
                               {"difficulty":"easy","example_id": f"fs_norm_{i:04d}", "error_type": wrong_type}))

# 3) Regulator minimal pairs & hard negatives (400)
# We'll craft scenarios that are close, but map to different regulators.
pairs = [
    # FM-Female vs FM-Male
    ("I don't know who/what to learn this from", "FM-Female",
     "I don't know what larger set this idea belongs to", "FM-Male"),
    # ACBE vs FM-Male
    ("I need an ability/capability I can't name yet", "ACBE",
     "I need to know what related topics surround this ability", "FM-Male"),
    # MVR vs FM-Female
    ("I have zero motivation/desire for this idea", "MVR",
     "I want to do this but don't know who to model/learn from", "FM-Female"),
    # MVR vs ACBE
    ("I know what to do but I can't get myself to care", "MVR",
     "I care a lot but I'm missing a key capability to proceed", "ACBE"),
]
for i in range(200):
    a_prompt, a_ans, b_prompt, b_ans = random.choice(pairs)
    # Randomize order
    if random.random() < 0.5:
        s1, ans1, s2, ans2 = a_prompt, a_ans, b_prompt, b_ans
    else:
        s1, ans1, s2, ans2 = b_prompt, b_ans, a_prompt, a_ans
    prompt = (
        "Two similar scenarios. For each, choose the correct regulator: FM-Female, FM-Male, ACBE, or MVR. "
        "Output two lines: '1) <reg>' and '2) <reg>'.\n"
        f"1) {s1}\n"
        f"2) {s2}"
    )
    completion = f"1) {ans1}\n2) {ans2}"
    records.append(make_record("regulator_minimal_pairs", prompt, completion,
                               {"difficulty":"medium","example_id": f"reg_pair_{i:04d}"}))

# 4) Regulator trigger classification with hard negatives (200)
# Create scenario with distractor text; classify correct regulator.
def make_scenario():
    base = random.choice([
        ("FM-Female","I don't know who/what to learn this from"),
        ("FM-Male","I don't know what larger set this belongs to"),
        ("ACBE","I need an unknown capability/ability to proceed"),
        ("MVR","I have no desire/motivation for this idea")
    ])
    target, core = base
    distractors = [
        "I feel some resistance but mostly it's unclear what to do",
        "There are many related topics but I can't see the big picture",
        "I have sources but none seem trustworthy",
        "I know the steps but I'm procrastinating",
        "I can do parts of it but something is missing",
    ]
    # Add 0-2 distractor sentences that should NOT change the regulator if core is clear.
    extra = " ".join(random.sample(distractors, k=random.randint(0,2)))
    return target, (core + ((" " + extra) if extra else ""))

for i in range(200):
    target, scenario = make_scenario()
    prompt = (
        "Choose which regulator activates based on the PRIMARY issue:\n"
        "- FM-Female: missing authority/reservoir\n"
        "- FM-Male: missing related-set/superset\n"
        "- ACBE: missing unknown capability (cause/effect analysis)\n"
        "- MVR: no desire/motivation (Mind/Vibration/Rhythm activation)\n"
        "Output ONLY the regulator name.\n"
        f"Scenario: {scenario}"
    )
    completion = target
    records.append(make_record("regulator_trigger_classify_hardneg", prompt, completion,
                               {"difficulty":"easy","example_id": f"reg_cls_{i:04d}", "target": target}))

# 5) RPM case with 28 sub-foundation stacks (100)
# Create concise RPM tree output using provided stack and indicate trigger tags.
def trinity_placeholder():
    # Keep simple placeholders; real system can replace with true expressions
    return f"(X:{random.choice(['A','B','C','D'])}{random.randint(1,10)}:Q{random.randint(1,22)})"

triggers = ["FM-Female","FM-Male","ACBE","MVR","None"]
for i in range(100):
    goal = random.choice(idea_labels)
    stack = pick_stack(3)
    G = trinity_placeholder()
    P = [trinity_placeholder() for _ in range(3)]
    trig = random.choice(triggers)
    prompt = (
        "Create a 2–3 layer RPM sketch using the provided 28 sub-foundation stack. "
        "Output as a bullet tree. Add a '(trigger=...)' tag next to any prerequisite that should activate a regulator.\n"
        f"Goal (NL): {goal}\nStack: {stack}\nGoalExpr: {G}"
    )
    # Put trigger on P2 and add a sub-prereq under P2
    sub = trinity_placeholder()
    trig_tag = f" (trigger={trig})" if trig != "None" else ""
    completion = (
        f"- Goal {G} _{{{stack}}}\n"
        f"  - P1 {P[0]}\n"
        f"  - P2 {P[1]}{trig_tag}\n"
        f"    - P2.1 {sub}\n"
        f"  - P3 {P[2]}"
    )
    records.append(make_record("rpm_case_with_subfoundations", prompt, completion,
                               {"difficulty":"medium","example_id": f"rpm_sf_{i:04d}", "stack": stack, "trigger": trig}))

len(records)

    for r in records:

from pathlib import Path

# Canonical 40 element naming reconstructed from TKS calculus structure
aspect = {
    1:"Mind", 2:"Positive", 3:"Negative", 4:"Vibration", 5:"Female", 6:"Male", 7:"Rhythm", 8:"Above/Cause", 9:"Below/Effect", 10:"Idea"
}
world_adj = {"A":"Spiritual","B":"Mental","C":"Emotional","D":"Physical"}

elements = []
element_label = {}
for w in ["A","B","C","D"]:
    for n in range(1,11):
        sym = f"{w}{n}"
        elements.append(sym)
        element_label[sym] = f"{world_adj[w]} {aspect[n]}"

worlds = {"A":"Spiritual World","B":"Mental World","C":"Emotional World","D":"Physical World"}

ops = {
    "+": {"name":"Association","verb":"associates with","meaning":"link/combine so both ideas coexist/support"},
    "-": {"name":"Disassociation","verb":"removes from","meaning":"remove/sever the second idea’s influence from the first"},
    "×": {"name":"Intensification","verb":"intensifies through","meaning":"amplify/fuse the interaction to strengthen effect"},
    "÷": {"name":"Opposition","verb":"opposes","meaning":"set in contrast/polarity to highlight tension"}
}

sub_foundations = [f"{m}{w}" for m in range(1,8) for w in ["a","b","c","d"]]  # 1a..7d

noetic_names = {
    "Mind":"^1", "Positive":"^2", "Negative":"^3", "Vibration":"^4", "Female":"^5", "Male":"^6",
    "Rhythm":"^7", "Above/Cause":"^8", "Below/Effect":"^9", "Idea":"^0"
}

def make_record(rec_type, prompt, completion, meta, system=None):
    if system is None:
        system = "You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Use canonical TKS element names and operator semantics. Keep outputs concise."
    return {
        "type": rec_type,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content": system},
            {"role":"user","content": prompt},
            {"role":"assistant","content": completion}
        ]
    }

# ---------------- Pack 4: Calculus Canonical Training (1500) ----------------
pack4 = []

# A) World grounding (80)
for i in range(80):
    w = random.choice(list(worlds.keys()))
    prompt = f"What does the world letter '{w}' stand for in TKS? Answer in one short sentence."
    completion = f"'{w}' denotes the {worlds[w]} domain."
    pack4.append(make_record("world_grounding", prompt, completion, {"example_id": f"w_{i:03d}", "world": w}))

# B) Element name grounding (40 elements * 5 = 200)
for sym in elements:
    for j in range(5):
        prompt = f"Give the canonical name for the TKS element {sym} and one short paraphrase of what it points to."
        completion = f"{sym} = {element_label[sym]}. It points to {world_adj[sym[0]].lower()}-domain {aspect[int(sym[1:])].lower()} dynamics."
        pack4.append(make_record("element_name_grounding", prompt, completion,
                                 {"example_id": f"e_{sym}_{j:02d}", "element": sym}))

# C) Operator grounding with canonical operands (200)
for op in ops:
    for i in range(50):
        X, Y = random.choice(elements), random.choice(elements)
        prompt = f"Define operator '{op}' (one clause). Then interpret:\n{X} {op} {Y}"
        completion = f"'{op}' = {ops[op]['name']}: {ops[op]['meaning']}. {element_label[X]} {ops[op]['verb']} {element_label[Y]}."
        pack4.append(make_record("operator_grounding_canonical", prompt, completion,
                                 {"example_id": f"opg_{op}_{i:03d}", "operator": op, "X": X, "Y": Y}))

# D) Calculus-style title interpretation (600)
# Create titles like: "<LABEL (X)> + <WORLD (W)>"
for i in range(600):
    X = random.choice(elements)
    W = random.choice(list(worlds.keys()))
    op = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    title = f"{element_label[X].upper()} ({X}) {op} {worlds[W].upper()} ({W})"
    prompt = (
        "Convert this calculus-style heading into a compact TKS expression, then give a 1-sentence interpretation.\n"
        f"Heading: {title}"
    )
    completion = f"Expr: {X} {op} {W}. Meaning: {element_label[X]} {ops[op]['verb']} the {worlds[W]} context."
    pack4.append(make_record("calculus_heading_interpret", prompt, completion,
                             {"example_id": f"head_{i:04d}", "operator": op, "element": X, "world": W}))

# E) Minimal pairs (same operands, swap ops) (300)
for g in range(300):
    X, Y = random.choice(elements), random.choice(elements)
    prompt = (
        "Same operands, different operators. Provide 4 short bullets showing how meaning changes.\n"
        f"X={X} ({element_label[X]})\nY={Y} ({element_label[Y]})\nUse +, -, ×, ÷"
    )
    completion = "\n".join([
        f"- + : {element_label[X]} associates with {element_label[Y]}.",
        f"- - : {element_label[X]} removes {element_label[Y]}.",
        f"- × : {element_label[X]} intensifies through {element_label[Y]}.",
        f"- ÷ : {element_label[X]} opposes {element_label[Y]}."
    ])
    pack4.append(make_record("operator_minimal_pair_canonical", prompt, completion,
                             {"example_id": f"minc_{g:04d}", "X": X, "Y": Y}))

# F) Operator selection from NL intent with canonical operands (120)
intent_cues = {
    "+": ["link these so they cooperate","combine so both are present","associate the two ideas"],
    "-": ["remove the second influence from the first","sever the link to the second","disassociate the second from the first"],
    "×": ["amplify the effect by combining them strongly","intensify the first through the second","make the interaction stronger"],
    "÷": ["contrast them to highlight tension","set them in polarity/opposition","show the contradiction between them"]
}
for i in range(120):
    X, Y = random.choice(elements), random.choice(elements)
    op = random.choice(list(ops.keys()))
    cue = random.choice(intent_cues[op])
    prompt = (
        "Choose the correct operator (+, -, ×, ÷) that matches the intent and output ONLY the completed expression.\n"
        f"Intent: {cue}\nX={X} ({element_label[X]})\nY={Y} ({element_label[Y]})"
    )
    completion = f"{X} {op} {Y}"
    pack4.append(make_record("operator_selection_canonical", prompt, completion,
                             {"example_id": f"ops_c_{i:03d}", "operator": op, "X": X, "Y": Y}))

    1:"Unity/Alignment with Source",

    3:"Life/Vitality/Health",

    5:"Worldly Power/Influence/Control (Efficiency)",

    7:"Lust/Sex/Reproduction (Creation/Generativity)"

def pick_goal_for_foundation(m):
    samples = {
        1:["align system actions to highest values","reduce harmful drift and stay principled","maintain coherence and purpose"],
        2:["learn the domain to reduce uncertainty","build a reliable knowledge base","increase understanding and clarity"],
        3:["stabilize performance and reduce errors","improve uptime/health of system","increase energy and resilience"],
        4:["improve collaboration and trust","increase rapport with users","strengthen cooperative behavior"],
        5:["optimize efficiency and capability","increase leverage and control over execution","maximize throughput per cost"],
        6:["acquire needed tools/data/resources","improve infrastructure and inputs","secure materials/budget/time"],
        7:["generate novel solutions and iterate","create new artifacts/tools","increase creative output"]
    }
    return random.choice(samples[m])

def make_stack(primary_m, depth=3, align_constraint=True):
    # choose manifestation letters
    letters = ["a","b","c","d"]
    primary = f"{primary_m}{random.choice(letters)}"
    secondary = random.choice(sub_foundations)
    if align_constraint:
        tertiary = f"1{random.choice(letters)}"
    else:
        tertiary = random.choice(sub_foundations)
    return f"{primary}:{secondary}:{tertiary}"

def trinity_expr_from_foundation(primary_m):
    # Choose an element matching primary world loosely (just for realism)
    w = random.choice(["A","B","C","D"])
    n = random.randint(1,10)
    element = f"{w}{n}"
    # Use Idea or Mind superscripts often for goals
    sup = random.choice(["^0","^1"])
    stack = make_stack(primary_m, 3, align_constraint=True)
    return f"{element}{sup}_{{{stack}}}"

    prompt = (
        "Create a TKS goal configuration for the given foundation. "
        "Output 3 lines: Stack:, GoalExpr:, GoalMeaning: (1 sentence). "

        f"Stack: {stack}\n"
        f"GoalExpr: {goal_expr}\n"
        f"GoalMeaning: Serve F{m} by pursuing the goal within the chosen stack context."
    )
    pack5.append(make_record("foundation_goal_config", prompt, completion,
                             {"example_id": f"fgc_{i:04d}", "foundation": f"F{m}", "stack": stack},

    P_wis = trinity_expr_from_foundation(2)  # wisdom style

import re, json, random, textwrap
from pathlib import Path
import PyPDF2

random.seed(999)

pdf_path = Path("/mnt/data/BOOK OF KABBALISTIC CALCULUS W 40 E DEFS.pdf")
reader = PyPDF2.PdfReader(str(pdf_path))

# Canonical labels (reconstructed from TKS structure)
aspect = {1:"Mind",2:"Positive",3:"Negative",4:"Vibration",5:"Female",6:"Male",7:"Rhythm",8:"Above/Cause",9:"Below/Effect",10:"Idea"}
world_adj = {"A":"Spiritual","B":"Mental","C":"Emotional","D":"Physical"}
def element_label(sym:str)->str:
    w = sym[0]
    n = int(sym[1:])
    return f"{world_adj[w]} {aspect[n]}"

op_map = {
    "+": ("Association","associates with"),
    "-": ("Disassociation","removes from"),
    "×": ("Intensification","intensifies through"),
    "x": ("Intensification","intensifies through"),
    "÷": ("Opposition","opposes"),
    "/": ("Opposition","opposes"),
}

# Regex to capture equation+description blocks
pat = re.compile(r"(?m)([ABCD]\d{1,2}\s*[\+\-×x/÷]\s*[ABCD]\d{1,2})\s*:\s*(.*?)(?=\n\s*\d+\.\s|\Z)", re.DOTALL)

def clean_equation(eq):
    eq = re.sub(r"\s+", "", eq)
    eq = eq.replace("x","×").replace("/","÷")
    return eq

def clean_text(t):
    # remove odd hyphen breaks and collapse whitespace
    t = t.replace("\n-\n","")
    t = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", t).strip()
    return t

pairs = []
max_pairs = 2500  # cap extraction for speed & size
for i in range(len(reader.pages)):
    txt = reader.pages[i].extract_text() or ""
    if "A1" not in txt and "+" not in txt and "×" not in txt and "x" not in txt and "-" not in txt and "÷" not in txt and "/" not in txt:
        continue
    for eq, desc in pat.findall(txt):
        eqc = clean_equation(eq)
        desc_c = clean_text(desc)
        # basic sanity
        m = re.match(r"^([ABCD]\d{1,2})([\+\-×÷])([ABCD]\d{1,2})$", eqc)
        if not m:
            continue
        a, op, b = m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)
        # store tiny snippet only (<=20 words)
        snippet_words = desc_c.split(" ")[:20]
        snippet = " ".join(snippet_words)
        pairs.append({
            "equation": eqc,
            "a": a,
            "op": op,
            "b": b,
            "page_index": i,
            "snippet": snippet
        })
        if len(pairs) >= max_pairs:
            break
    if len(pairs) >= max_pairs:
        break

len(pairs), pairs[0] if pairs else None

    eq = p["equation"]
    if eq not in eq_best:
        eq_best[eq] = p
    else:
        # prefer one with longer snippet (more informative)
        if len(p["snippet"]) > len(eq_best[eq]["snippet"]):
            eq_best[eq] = p

def make_record(rec_type, prompt, completion, meta, system=None):
    if system is None:
        system = "You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Use canonical element labels and operator semantics. Keep outputs concise."
    return {
        "type": rec_type,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content": system},
            {"role":"user","content": prompt},
            {"role":"assistant","content": completion}
        ]
    }

# Helper for structured meaning
def meaning_sentence(a, op, b):
    op_name, verb = op_map[op][0], op_map[op][1]
    return f"{element_label(a)} {verb} {element_label(b)} ({op_name})."

# 1) equation -> structured meaning + short book snippet (1000)
for i in range(1000):
    p = random.choice(unique)
    eq, a, op, b = p["equation"], p["a"], p["op"], p["b"]
    prompt = f"Interpret this calculus equation in one sentence using canonical element labels:\n{eq}"
    completion = f"{meaning_sentence(a,op,b)} Note (book snippet): {p['snippet']}"
    records6.append(make_record("calculus_eq_to_meaning_real", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"eq2m_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "page_index": p["page_index"], "operator": op}))

# 2) meaning -> equation (choose operands/op) (600)
for i in range(600):
    p = random.choice(unique)
    eq, a, op, b = p["equation"], p["a"], p["op"], p["b"]
    prompt = (
        "Output the equation (format: X<op>Y) that matches the meaning. Output ONLY the equation.\n"
        f"Meaning: {element_label(a)} {op_map[op][1]} {element_label(b)}"
    )
    completion = eq
    records6.append(make_record("calculus_meaning_to_eq_real", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"m2eq_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "operator": op}))

# 3) operator identification (500)
for i in range(500):
    p = random.choice(unique)
    eq, op = p["equation"], p["op"]
    prompt = (
        "Identify the operator used in the equation. Output ONLY one of: +, -, ×, ÷.\n"
        f"Equation: {eq}"
    )
    completion = op
    records6.append(make_record("calculus_operator_identify_real", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"opid_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "operator": op}))

# 4) operand label extraction (400)
for i in range(400):
    p = random.choice(unique)
    eq, a, b = p["equation"], p["a"], p["b"]
    prompt = (
        "Extract operand labels. Output two lines:\n"
        "Left: <label>\nRight: <label>\n"
        f"Equation: {eq}"
    )
    completion = f"Left: {element_label(a)}\nRight: {element_label(b)}"
    records6.append(make_record("calculus_operand_labels_real", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"olab_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "left": a, "right": b}))

# 5) minimal pair: swap operator, explain shift (500)
ops_list = ["+","-","×","÷"]
for i in range(500):
    p = random.choice(unique)
    a, b = p["a"], p["b"]
    op1 = random.choice(ops_list)
    op2 = random.choice([o for o in ops_list if o != op1])
    eq1 = f"{a}{op1}{b}"
    eq2 = f"{a}{op2}{b}"
    prompt = (
        "Explain the difference between these two equations in 2 short sentences.\n"
        f"1) {eq1}\n2) {eq2}"
    )
    completion = (
        f"1) {meaning_sentence(a,op1,b)}\n"
        f"2) {meaning_sentence(a,op2,b)}"
    )
    records6.append(make_record("calculus_minpair_op_swap_realstyle", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"minsw_{i:04d}", "left": a, "right": b, "op1": op1, "op2": op2}))

records7 = []

# Sub-foundation stack helper
sub_foundations = [f"{m}{w}" for m in range(1,8) for w in ["a","b","c","d"]]
def pick_stack(depth=3):
    return ":".join(random.choice(sub_foundations) for _ in range(depth))

# Regulator triggers based on node type text in prompt
# We'll create: goal + 3 prereqs + one deeper sub-prereq, using real equations for each node.

    p = random.choice(unique)
    return p["equation"], p["op"], p["a"], p["b"], p["page_index"]

    "1":"Unity/Alignment","2":"Wisdom/Knowledge","3":"Vitality/Health","4":"Love/Connection",
    "5":"Power/Efficiency","6":"Resources/Material","7":"Creation/Generativity"
}

# 1) Generate RPM trees with trigger tags (600)

    p1_eq, _, _, _, _ = pick_eq()
    p2_eq, _, _, _, _ = pick_eq()
    p3_eq, _, _, _, _ = pick_eq()
    sub_eq, _, _, _, _ = pick_eq()

    prompt = (

        "Use colon nesting Option A for the foundation stack only. "
        "Output a bullet tree. Add a trigger tag on the prerequisite that requires it.\n"

        "- FM-Male: missing related set/superset\n"
        "- ACBE: unknown capability needed\n"
        "- MVR: no desire/motivation"
    )

            "Rhythm: repeat via daily micro-step plus weekly review."

                                {"example_id": f"regEx_{i:04d}", "trigger": trig, "page": pg},
                                system="You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Apply the correct regulator expansion template concisely."))

from pathlib import Path
import math, json

pack6 = Path("/mnt/data/tks_calculus_REAL_equations_pack_3000.jsonl")
pack7 = Path("/mnt/data/tks_rpm_REAL_equations_pack_1200.jsonl")

def split_jsonl(path: Path, records_per_part: int, prefix: str):
    lines = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
    parts = []
    n = len(lines)
    k = math.ceil(n / records_per_part)
    for i in range(k):
        part_lines = lines[i*records_per_part:(i+1)*records_per_part]
        out = Path(f"/mnt/data/{prefix}_part{i+1:02d}_of_{k:02d}.jsonl")
        out.write_text("\n".join(part_lines) + ("\n" if part_lines else ""), encoding="utf-8")
        parts.append(out)
    return parts, n

parts6, n6 = split_jsonl(pack6, 500, "tks_calculus_REAL_equations_pack_3000")
parts7, n7 = split_jsonl(pack7, 300, "tks_rpm_REAL_equations_pack_1200")

Got it — if downloads aren’t working and it’s not size, the clean workaround is: regenerate the packs locally from your PDF (so you don’t need any sandbox links at all).

Below is a single Python script that:

extracts real equations from BOOK OF KABBALISTIC CALCULUS W 40 E DEFS.pdf

builds Pack 6 (3,000 records) + Pack 7 (1,200 records)

optionally splits them into parts

You run it on your machine where the PDF is. It will output the JSONL files directly.

# tks_build_packs_from_calculus_pdf.py
# pip install PyPDF2

import re, json, random, math
from pathlib import Path
import PyPDF2

random.seed(1001)

# ---- CONFIG ----
PDF_PATH = Path("BOOK OF KABBALISTIC CALCULUS W 40 E DEFS.pdf")  # <- set path
OUT_DIR = Path("./tks_packs_out")
OUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

PACK6_N = 3000
PACK7_N = 1200

SPLIT_PACK6_EVERY = 500   # set None to disable splitting
SPLIT_PACK7_EVERY = 300   # set None to disable splitting
# ---------------

aspect = {1:"Mind",2:"Positive",3:"Negative",4:"Vibration",5:"Female",6:"Male",7:"Rhythm",8:"Above/Cause",9:"Below/Effect",10:"Idea"}
world_adj = {"A":"Spiritual","B":"Mental","C":"Emotional","D":"Physical"}

def element_label(sym: str) -> str:
    w = sym[0]
    n = int(sym[1:])
    return f"{world_adj[w]} {aspect[n]}"

op_map = {
    "+": ("Association","associates with","link/combine so both ideas coexist/support"),
    "-": ("Disassociation","removes from","remove/sever the second idea’s influence from the first"),
    "×": ("Intensification","intensifies through","amplify/fuse the interaction to strengthen effect"),
    "÷": ("Opposition","opposes","set in contrast/polarity to highlight tension"),
}

# Match: A1+A2: description...
pat = re.compile(
    r"(?m)([ABCD]\d{1,2}\s*[\+\-×x/÷]\s*[ABCD]\d{1,2})\s*:\s*(.*?)(?=\n\s*\d+\.\s|\Z)",
    re.DOTALL
)

def clean_equation(eq: str) -> str:
    eq = re.sub(r"\s+", "", eq)
    eq = eq.replace("x","×").replace("/","÷")
    return eq

def clean_text(t: str) -> str:
    t = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", t).strip()
    return t

def meaning_sentence(a, op, b):
    op_name, verb, _ = op_map[op]
    return f"{element_label(a)} {verb} {element_label(b)} ({op_name})."

def make_record(rec_type, prompt, completion, meta, system):
    return {
        "type": rec_type,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content": system},
            {"role":"user","content": prompt},
            {"role":"assistant","content": completion},
        ]
    }

def extract_equations(pdf_path: Path, max_pairs=50000):
    reader = PyPDF2.PdfReader(str(pdf_path))
    pairs = []
    for i in range(len(reader.pages)):
        txt = reader.pages[i].extract_text() or ""
        for eq, desc in pat.findall(txt):
            eqc = clean_equation(eq)
            desc_c = clean_text(desc)

            m = re.match(r"^([ABCD]\d{1,2})([\+\-×÷])([ABCD]\d{1,2})$", eqc)
            if not m:
                continue
            a, op, b = m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)
            if op not in op_map:
                continue

            snippet = " ".join(desc_c.split()[:20])
            pairs.append({"equation": eqc, "a": a, "op": op, "b": b, "page_index": i, "snippet": snippet})
            if len(pairs) >= max_pairs:
                return pairs
    return pairs

def dedupe_best(pairs):
    best = {}
    for p in pairs:
        eq = p["equation"]
        if eq not in best or len(p["snippet"]) > len(best[eq]["snippet"]):
            best[eq] = p
    return list(best.values())

def write_jsonl(path: Path, records):
    with path.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for r in records:
            f.write(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

def split_jsonl(path: Path, every: int):
    lines = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
    k = math.ceil(len(lines)/every)
    out_paths = []
    for i in range(k):
        part = lines[i*every:(i+1)*every]
        out = path.with_name(f"{path.stem}_part{i+1:02d}_of_{k:02d}{path.suffix}")
        out.write_text("\n".join(part) + ("\n" if part else ""), encoding="utf-8")
        out_paths.append(out)
    return out_paths

def build_pack6(unique, n=3000):
    system = "You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Use canonical element labels and operator semantics. Keep outputs concise."
    recs = []
    ops_list = ["+","-","×","÷"]

    # 1) equation -> meaning + snippet (about 1/3)
    for i in range(n//3):
        p = random.choice(unique)
        eq,a,op,b = p["equation"], p["a"], p["op"], p["b"]
        prompt = f"Interpret this calculus equation in one sentence using canonical element labels:\n{eq}"
        completion = f"{meaning_sentence(a,op,b)} Note (book snippet): {p['snippet']}"
        recs.append(make_record("calculus_eq_to_meaning_real", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"eq2m_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "page_index": p["page_index"], "operator": op}, system))

    # 2) meaning -> equation (about 1/5)
    for i in range(n//5):
        p = random.choice(unique)
        eq,a,op,b = p["equation"], p["a"], p["op"], p["b"]
        prompt = ("Output the equation (format: X<op>Y) that matches the meaning. Output ONLY the equation.\n"
                  f"Meaning: {element_label(a)} {op_map[op][1]} {element_label(b)}")
        recs.append(make_record("calculus_meaning_to_eq_real", prompt, eq,
                                {"example_id": f"m2eq_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "operator": op}, system))

    # 3) operator identify (about 1/6)
    for i in range(n//6):
        p = random.choice(unique)
        eq,op = p["equation"], p["op"]
        prompt = "Identify the operator used in the equation. Output ONLY one of: +, -, ×, ÷.\n" + f"Equation: {eq}"
        recs.append(make_record("calculus_operator_identify_real", prompt, op,
                                {"example_id": f"opid_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "operator": op}, system))

    # 4) operand labels (about 1/8)
    for i in range(n//8):
        p = random.choice(unique)
        eq,a,b = p["equation"], p["a"], p["b"]
        prompt = "Extract operand labels. Output two lines:\nLeft: <label>\nRight: <label>\n" + f"Equation: {eq}"
        completion = f"Left: {element_label(a)}\nRight: {element_label(b)}"
        recs.append(make_record("calculus_operand_labels_real", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"olab_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "left": a, "right": b}, system))

    # 5) minimal pair swap operator (fill remaining)
    while len(recs) < n:
        p = random.choice(unique)
        a,b = p["a"], p["b"]
        op1 = random.choice(ops_list)
        op2 = random.choice([o for o in ops_list if o != op1])
        eq1, eq2 = f"{a}{op1}{b}", f"{a}{op2}{b}"
        prompt = f"Explain the difference between these two equations in 2 short sentences.\n1) {eq1}\n2) {eq2}"
        completion = f"1) {meaning_sentence(a,op1,b)}\n2) {meaning_sentence(a,op2,b)}"
        recs.append(make_record("calculus_minpair_op_swap_realstyle", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"minsw_{len(recs):04d}", "left": a, "right": b, "op1": op1, "op2": op2}, system))
    return recs[:n]

def build_pack7(unique, n=1200):
    system_tree = "You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Build RPM trees using real calculus equations and correct trigger tags. Keep outputs concise."
    system_cls  = "You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Choose the correct regulator strictly from the issue type."
    system_exp  = "You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Apply the correct regulator expansion template concisely."

    sub_foundations = [f"{m}{w}" for m in range(1,8) for w in ["a","b","c","d"]]
    def pick_stack(depth=3):
        return ":".join(random.choice(sub_foundations) for _ in range(depth))

    foundation_names = {
        "1":"Unity/Alignment","2":"Wisdom/Knowledge","3":"Vitality/Health","4":"Love/Connection",
        "5":"Power/Efficiency","6":"Resources/Material","7":"Creation/Generativity"
    }
    def pick_eq():
        p = random.choice(unique)
        return p["equation"], p["page_index"]

    recs = []

    # 1) RPM trees (half)
    for i in range(n//2):
        primary = str(random.randint(1,7))
        stack = pick_stack(3)
        goal_eq, pg = pick_eq()
        p1_eq, _ = pick_eq()
        p2_eq, _ = pick_eq()
        p3_eq, _ = pick_eq()
        sub_eq, _ = pick_eq()
        trig = random.choice(["FM-Female","FM-Male","ACBE","MVR","None"])
        trig_tag = f" (trigger={trig})" if trig != "None" else ""
        prompt = (
            "Generate a 2–3 layer RPM cascade using REAL calculus equations as node expressions. "
            "Use colon nesting Option A for the foundation stack only. "
            "Output a bullet tree. Add a trigger tag on the prerequisite that requires it.\n"
            f"Primary Foundation: F{primary} ({foundation_names[primary]})\n"
            f"Stack: {stack}\n"
            f"GoalExpr: {goal_eq}\n"
            "Trigger rules:\n"
            "- FM-Female: missing authority/reservoir\n"
            "- FM-Male: missing related set/superset\n"
            "- ACBE: unknown capability needed\n"
            "- MVR: no desire/motivation"
        )
        completion = (
            f"- Goal {goal_eq} _{{{stack}}}\n"
            f"  - P1 {p1_eq}\n"
            f"  - P2 {p2_eq}{trig_tag}\n"
            f"    - P2.1 {sub_eq}\n"
            f"  - P3 {p3_eq}"
        )
        recs.append(make_record("rpm_tree_real_equations", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"rpmR_{i:04d}", "foundation": f"F{primary}", "stack": stack, "goal_page": pg, "trigger": trig},
                                system_tree))

    # 2) trigger choice (quarter)
    scenarios = [
        ("FM-Female","Wisdom prerequisite: I don't know who/what to learn this from."),
        ("FM-Male","Wisdom prerequisite: I don't know what larger set of ideas this belongs to."),
        ("ACBE","Capability prerequisite: I need an unknown ability to proceed."),
        ("MVR","Activation prerequisite: I have no desire/motivation to act on this."),
    ]
    for i in range(n//4):
        target, sc = random.choice(scenarios)
        eq, pg = pick_eq()
        prompt = ("Given the prerequisite issue and its expression, choose the correct regulator. "
                  "Output ONLY: FM-Female, FM-Male, ACBE, or MVR.\n"
                  f"PrereqExpr: {eq}\nIssue: {sc}")
        recs.append(make_record("rpm_trigger_choice_real_equation", prompt, target,
                                {"example_id": f"trR_{i:04d}", "target": target, "page": pg},
                                system_cls))

    # 3) regulator expansions (fill remaining)
    while len(recs) < n:
        eq, pg = pick_eq()
        trig = random.choice(["FM-Female","FM-Male","ACBE","MVR"])
        if trig == "FM-Female":
            prompt = "FM-Female expansion for this prerequisite. Output 3 generic authority/reservoir candidates (no real people).\n" + f"PrereqExpr: {eq}"
            completion = "Candidates: (1) standard/reference text, (2) institutional guideline, (3) vetted practitioner model."
        elif trig == "FM-Male":
            prompt = "FM-Male expansion for this prerequisite. Output 5 related ideas that form its larger set.\n" + f"PrereqExpr: {eq}"
            completion = "Related: prerequisites, adjacent skills, tools/techniques, evaluation metrics, common failure modes."
        elif trig == "ACBE":
            prompt = "ACBE expansion for an unknown capability behind this prerequisite. Output 3 causes and 3 effects.\n" + f"PrereqExpr: {eq}"
            completion = "Causes: training/practice; enabling tools/resources; foundational knowledge. Effects: higher success; fewer errors; faster progress toward the desire."
        else:
            prompt = "MVR expansion for low desire around this prerequisite. Output 3 lines: Mind:, Vibration:, Rhythm:.\n" + f"PrereqExpr: {eq}"
            completion = ("Mind: define one clear next action and focus on it.\n"
                          "Vibration: connect it to a meaningful payoff and consequence.\n"
                          "Rhythm: repeat via daily micro-step plus weekly review.")
        recs.append(make_record("regulator_expand_real_equation", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"regEx_{len(recs):04d}", "trigger": trig, "page": pg},
                                system_exp))
    return recs[:n]

def main():
    if not PDF_PATH.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"PDF not found: {PDF_PATH.resolve()}")

    print("Extracting equations...")
    pairs = extract_equations(PDF_PATH, max_pairs=50000)
    if not pairs:
        raise RuntimeError("No equations extracted. Check PDF text extraction / pattern.")
    unique = dedupe_best(pairs)
    print(f"Extracted {len(pairs)} equation blocks; {len(unique)} unique equations.")

    print("Building Pack 6...")
    pack6 = build_pack6(unique, PACK6_N)
    p6_path = OUT_DIR / f"tks_calculus_REAL_equations_pack_{PACK6_N}.jsonl"
    write_jsonl(p6_path, pack6)
    print("Wrote:", p6_path)

    print("Building Pack 7...")
    pack7 = build_pack7(unique, PACK7_N)
    p7_path = OUT_DIR / f"tks_rpm_REAL_equations_pack_{PACK7_N}.jsonl"
    write_jsonl(p7_path, pack7)
    print("Wrote:", p7_path)

    if SPLIT_PACK6_EVERY:
        parts = split_jsonl(p6_path, SPLIT_PACK6_EVERY)
        print("Split Pack 6 into:", len(parts), "parts")
    if SPLIT_PACK7_EVERY:
        parts = split_jsonl(p7_path, SPLIT_PACK7_EVERY)
        print("Split Pack 7 into:", len(parts), "parts")

    print("Done.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
