1) TKS_Tootra_Blueprint_and_Dataset_Spec.md (copy/paste)
# TKS / Tootra Nesting-RPM-AI Blueprint + Dataset Build Spec

Generated: 2026-01-04 03:44 UTC

## 0) Purpose
This document specifies a **shared shorthand system** (equations/formulas) for modeling ideas/states/occurrences such that **two trained readers (or agents)** using the same definitions converge on the same interpretation. It also specifies a **training-data layering plan** to teach an AI model: (1) symbol grounding, (2) operator semantics, (3) nesting/fractal Noetics, (4) 7 Foundations + 28 Sub-Foundations stacks, and (5) RPM cascades regulated by FM / ACBE / MVR.

---

## 1) Core nesting rule (Option A)
**Colon nesting is containment**:

- `Outer:Inner` means **Inner is a sub-structure/sub-goal/sub-tree inside Outer**.
- `A:B:C` means `A:(B:C)` i.e., **A contains (B contains C)**.
- Practical depth limit: **2–3 layers** (infinite in theory, bounded in practice).

### 1.1 Trinity / step unit (for RPM nodes)
A single RPM node can be stored as a “trinity” (expand as needed):

- `(Noetic : Element : Acquisition)`
- Acquisition is `Q1..Q22` (placeholder if you haven’t attached full acquisition mapping yet).

### 1.2 Foundation stack as nested context
Every node is tagged by a **3-layer** Sub-Foundation stack:

- `mf:mf:mf` where `m ∈ 1..7` and `f ∈ {a,b,c,d}` (28 sub-foundations total)
- Read as: **Primary mission : method/lens : constraint/support**
- Recommended default constraint: `1a..1d` (alignment) as the 3rd layer.

Example: `1a:5b:2b` = align first, optimize efficiency, constrained by wisdom.

---

## 2) RPM as recursive planning (tree-of-trees)
**RPM** decomposes a prime desire/goal into prerequisites:

- Goal `G` → `RPM(G) = {P1, P2, …}`
- Each prerequisite can itself be expanded: `RPM(Pi) = {Pi.1, Pi.2, …}`
- Therefore **every prerequisite node can have its own tree**.

### 2.1 Nesting-depth parallel
- **RPM depth** (how far you drilled down prerequisites)
  parallels
- **Noetic/Tree nesting depth** (tree-within-tree modeling).

In practice: each RPM “missing prerequisite” identifies the next depth layer you enter.

### 2.2 Stopping rules (avoid infinite recursion)
Stop expanding when any condition is met:
- prerequisite is satisfied,
- next step is actionable in the real world,
- marginal gains drop below threshold,
- depth limit reached (spawn a new subtree instead of deeper nesting).

---

## 3) Regulators (triggered subroutines)

### 3.1 FM = Female/Male Noetic (Wisdom prerequisite regulator)
FM triggers when a **Wisdom prerequisite** is uncertain/under-defined.

**Female (Noetic 5): Authority/Reservoir**
- Question: *“Who/what is a trusted reservoir/authority/expert/embodiment for X?”*
- Output becomes new child prerequisites (sources, standards, exemplars).

**Male (Noetic 6): Related-set / Superset**
- Question: *“What larger set of ideas does X belong to? What related ideas cluster around it?”*
- Output becomes new child prerequisites (neighbors, supersets, taxonomy links).

### 3.2 ACBE = Above/Cause & Below/Effect (unknown capability regulator)
ACBE triggers when the system detects an **unknown ability/capability is needed** to fulfill a desire.

**Above/Cause (Noetic 8):**
- *“What causes/precursors would produce this capability?”*

**Below/Effect (Noetic 9):**
- *“What are the effects/outcomes of those causes, and do they fulfill the desire?”*

Outputs: cause-chain prerequisites + effect-tests.

### 3.3 MVR = Mind, Vibration, Rhythm (desire activation regulator)
MVR triggers when there is **no desire/motivation** to act on an idea.

- **Mind (1):** allocate attention; define a clear next action.
- **Vibration (4):** intensify salience (payoff, consequence, embodiment).
- **Rhythm (7):** repeated exposure/practice schedule.

Outputs: activation prerequisites (attention → intensity → repetition).

---

## 4) Operators (+, −, ×, ÷) as relationship types
Operators are **semantic relationship operators** (not arithmetic).

- `+` Association: link/combine so both ideas coexist/support
- `−` Disassociation: remove/sever the second idea’s influence from the first
- `×` Intensification: amplify/fuse interaction to strengthen effect
- `÷` Opposition: contrast/polarity/tension

### 4.1 Substitution test (operator unit-test)
Validate operator choice by swapping the operator for a phrase:
- `+` → “together with / linked with”
- `−` → “remove/subtract influence of”
- `×` → “amplified by / intensified through”
- `÷` → “in tension with / contrasted against”

If the meaning holds, the operator is valid.

### 4.2 Precedence
Use **PEMDAS-style precedence**:
- Parentheses first,
- `×` and `÷` before `+` and `−`,
- left-to-right within a tier.

---

## 5) 7 Foundations as nested/fractal drive-system
Treat Foundations as **fractal drives**:
- Each Foundation contains all 7 Foundations as sub-drives,
- Use 2–3 layers for practical purposes.

Represent as:
- `Fi:Fj` = serve Fi using Fj (method)
- `Fi:Fj:Fk` = serve Fi using Fj constrained/supported by Fk

Recommended global discipline:
- Keep **F1 alignment** as primary or (at minimum) as 3rd-layer constraint.

---

## 6) Training data layering plan (curriculum)
Build datasets in layers to force convergence:

### Layer A — Ontology grounding
- 4 Worlds (A/B/C/D) examples
- 40 Elements: canonical definition + 10–40 positive examples + hard negatives
- 7 Foundations + 28 Sub-Foundations: definitions + signals for immediacy/neglect

### Layer B — Noetics + nesting
- each Noetic definition
- recognition examples
- 2–3 layer nesting examples using colon containment

### Layer C — Operators + parsing
- minimal pairs (same operands, different operator)
- substitution-test records
- precedence/grouping records

### Layer D — Kabbalistic Calculus corpus
- equation → canonical interpretation
- interpretation → equation
- tags: world(s), operator, element(s), foundation context

### Layer E — RPM cascades + regulators
- goal → prerequisite tree (2–3 layers)
- trigger tags for FM/ACBE/MVR
- stopping conditions

### Layer F — Natural language bridge
- user utterance → extracted (foundation stack, goal expression)
- expression → plain-language explanation
- multiple tones (technical vs plain) while keeping semantics fixed

---

## 7) Recommended canonical JSONL schema (SFT)
Each record:
- `type` (task type)
- `meta` (ids, difficulty, operator/foundation tags)
- `messages`: system/user/assistant

Example:
```json
{
  "type": "operator_selection",
  "meta": {"operator":"+","difficulty":"easy"},
  "messages": [
    {"role":"system","content":"..."},
    {"role":"user","content":"Intent: link these ideas..."},
    {"role":"assistant","content":"A1 + B2"}
  ]
}
8) Practical note for phone workflows
If you cannot download large artifacts on phone:

Send this MD/PDF to your agents, or

Agents regenerate JSONL packs locally from your PDFs using a Python extraction script.


---

## 2) `TKS_Pack_Builder_Scripts.md` (copy/paste)

```md
# TKS Pack Builder Scripts (Local Regeneration)

Generated: 2026-01-04 03:44 UTC

## Why this exists
If you are on mobile and cannot download JSONL artifacts, the simplest workflow is:
- **Send these scripts to your agents**, and
- Agents run them on a laptop/server that has the TKS PDFs.

This produces the same style of packs we discussed:
- operator packs
- regulator (FM/ACBE/MVR) packs
- 28 sub-foundation stack packs
- calculus-derived packs extracted from **BOOK OF KABBALISTIC CALCULUS W 40 E DEFS.pdf**
- RPM packs that use those real extracted equations as node expressions

---

## Script 1 — Build “real calculus equation” packs from the Calculus PDF

### Install
```bash
pip install PyPDF2
Run
python tks_build_packs_from_calculus_pdf.py
File: tks_build_packs_from_calculus_pdf.py
# pip install PyPDF2
import re, json, random
from pathlib import Path
import PyPDF2

random.seed(1001)

PDF_PATH = Path("BOOK OF KABBALISTIC CALCULUS W 40 E DEFS.pdf")  # <-- set this
OUT_DIR = Path("./tks_packs_out"); OUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

PACK6_N = 3000   # calculus real equations training pack
PACK7_N = 1200   # RPM/regulators using real equations

aspect = {1:"Mind",2:"Positive",3:"Negative",4:"Vibration",5:"Female",6:"Male",7:"Rhythm",8:"Above/Cause",9:"Below/Effect",10:"Idea"}
world_adj = {"A":"Spiritual","B":"Mental","C":"Emotional","D":"Physical"}

def element_label(sym: str) -> str:
    w = sym[0]; n = int(sym[1:])
    return f"{world_adj[w]} {aspect[n]}"

op_map = {
    "+": ("Association","associates with"),
    "-": ("Disassociation","removes from"),
    "×": ("Intensification","intensifies through"),
    "÷": ("Opposition","opposes"),
}

pat = re.compile(r"(?m)([ABCD]\d{1,2}\s*[\+\-×x/÷]\s*[ABCD]\d{1,2})\s*:\s*(.*?)(?=\n\s*\d+\.\s|\Z)", re.DOTALL)

def clean_equation(eq: str) -> str:
    eq = re.sub(r"\s+", "", eq)
    return eq.replace("x","×").replace("/","÷")

def clean_text(t: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", t).strip()

def make_record(rec_type, prompt, completion, meta, system):
    return {
        "type": rec_type,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content": system},
            {"role":"user","content": prompt},
            {"role":"assistant","content": completion},
        ]
    }

def extract_equations(pdf_path: Path, max_pairs=50000):
    reader = PyPDF2.PdfReader(str(pdf_path))
    pairs = []
    for i in range(len(reader.pages)):
        txt = reader.pages[i].extract_text() or ""
        for eq, desc in pat.findall(txt):
            eqc = clean_equation(eq)
            desc_c = clean_text(desc)
            m = re.match(r"^([ABCD]\d{1,2})([\+\-×÷])([ABCD]\d{1,2})$", eqc)
            if not m:
                continue
            a, op, b = m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)
            if op not in op_map:
                continue
            snippet = " ".join(desc_c.split()[:20])
            pairs.append({"equation": eqc, "a": a, "op": op, "b": b, "page_index": i, "snippet": snippet})
            if len(pairs) >= max_pairs:
                return pairs
    return pairs

def dedupe_best(pairs):
    best = {}
    for p in pairs:
        eq = p["equation"]
        if eq not in best or len(p["snippet"]) > len(best[eq]["snippet"]):
            best[eq] = p
    return list(best.values())

def write_jsonl(path: Path, records):
    with path.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for r in records:
            f.write(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

def meaning_sentence(a, op, b):
    return f"{element_label(a)} {op_map[op][1]} {element_label(b)}."

def build_pack6(unique, n):
    system = "You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Use canonical element labels and operator semantics. Keep outputs concise."
    recs = []
    ops_list = ["+","-","×","÷"]

    for i in range(n//3):
        p = random.choice(unique)
        eq,a,op,b = p["equation"], p["a"], p["op"], p["b"]
        prompt = f"Interpret this calculus equation in one sentence using canonical element labels:\n{eq}"
        completion = f"{meaning_sentence(a,op,b)} Note (book snippet): {p['snippet']}"
        recs.append(make_record("calculus_eq_to_meaning_real", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"eq2m_{i:04d}", "equation": eq, "page_index": p["page_index"], "operator": op}, system))

    while len(recs) < n:
        p = random.choice(unique); a,b = p["a"], p["b"]
        op1 = random.choice(ops_list); op2 = random.choice([o for o in ops_list if o != op1])
        eq1, eq2 = f"{a}{op1}{b}", f"{a}{op2}{b}"
        prompt = f"Explain the difference in 2 short sentences.\n1) {eq1}\n2) {eq2}"
        completion = f"1) {meaning_sentence(a,op1,b)}\n2) {meaning_sentence(a,op2,b)}"
        recs.append(make_record("calculus_minpair_op_swap_realstyle", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"minsw_{len(recs):04d}", "left": a, "right": b, "op1": op1, "op2": op2}, system))
    return recs[:n]

def build_pack7(unique, n):
    system_tree = "You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Build RPM trees using real calculus equations and correct trigger tags. Keep outputs concise."
    sub_foundations = [f"{m}{w}" for m in range(1,8) for w in ["a","b","c","d"]]
    def pick_stack(depth=3):
        return ":".join(random.choice(sub_foundations) for _ in range(depth))

    def pick_eq():
        p = random.choice(unique)
        return p["equation"], p["page_index"]

    recs = []
    for i in range(n):
        stack = pick_stack(3)
        goal_eq, pg = pick_eq()
        p1_eq, _ = pick_eq(); p2_eq, _ = pick_eq(); p3_eq, _ = pick_eq(); sub_eq, _ = pick_eq()
        trig = random.choice(["FM-Female","FM-Male","ACBE","MVR","None"])
        tag = f" (trigger={trig})" if trig != "None" else ""
        prompt = "Generate a 2–3 layer RPM cascade using REAL equations. Output a bullet tree.\n" + f"Stack: {stack}\nGoalExpr: {goal_eq}"
        completion = f"- Goal {goal_eq} _{{{stack}}}\n  - P1 {p1_eq}\n  - P2 {p2_eq}{tag}\n    - P2.1 {sub_eq}\n  - P3 {p3_eq}"
        recs.append(make_record("rpm_tree_real_equations", prompt, completion,
                                {"example_id": f"rpmR_{i:04d}", "stack": stack, "goal_page": pg, "trigger": trig}, system_tree))
    return recs[:n]

def main():
    if not PDF_PATH.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"PDF not found: {PDF_PATH.resolve()}")

    pairs = extract_equations(PDF_PATH)
    if not pairs:
        raise RuntimeError("No equations extracted. Check PDF text extraction.")
    unique = dedupe_best(pairs)

    pack6 = build_pack6(unique, PACK6_N)
    write_jsonl(OUT_DIR / f"tks_calculus_REAL_equations_pack_{PACK6_N}.jsonl", pack6)

    pack7 = build_pack7(unique, PACK7_N)
    write_jsonl(OUT_DIR / f"tks_rpm_REAL_equations_pack_{PACK7_N}.jsonl", pack7)

    print("Done. Output folder:", OUT_DIR)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
Script 2 — Convert markdown to PDF (optional)
VS Code “Markdown PDF” extension, or

Pandoc:

pandoc spec.md -o spec.pdf

---

If you tell me what your agents are using (**Windows / Mac / Linux / Colab**), I’ll adjust the script paths + run commands so it’s literally plug-and-play for them.
You said:
What else do I need to apart of this architecture for it to be the beginnings of a strong Ai?
Usora said:
If you want this to be the beginning of a strong AI, what you’ve built (Foundations → stacks → RPM → FM/ACBE/MVR → operators) is an excellent goal + reasoning layer. What’s missing is the rest of the “AI organism” around it: memory, grounding, tools, evaluation, and safety.

Here’s what you should add next (in priority order):

1) A clear base model + training strategy
Your TKS system is a framework. You still need a strong base model underneath:

Choose the base LLM (or your own transformer) + whether you’re doing SFT only first, then preference tuning / RL later.

Train in phases: symbols → operators → nesting → RPM → regulators → full dialogues (curriculum learning).

2) A state store (so it’s not “goldfish AI”)
You need persistent state, otherwise “self-sustaining” collapses.

Minimum memory modules:

Short-term working memory: current goal stack, current RPM frontier, active constraints.

Episodic memory: “what worked last time,” successes/failures, costs.

Semantic memory / ontology store: the canonical definitions, examples, hard negatives.

Practical implementation: vector DB + structured DB side-by-side.

3) Grounding + “reality hooks”
Right now it can become internally consistent but detached. To be strong, it needs feedback loops:

Tool access (search, code execution, file ops, etc.)

Environment signals (task success/failure, user feedback, time/cost)

“Proof” requirements for claims (at least internally): checklists, tests, validation steps.

4) A planner–executor split (agent loop)
RPM is a planner, but you need an executor that can actually do things.

A robust loop:

Select Foundation stack (Fi:Fj:Fk) using immediacy/neglect + memory

Generate/choose goal expression

RPM expand → prerequisites

Pick next actionable prerequisite

Run regulators when needed

FM if knowledge uncertain

ACBE if capability unknown

MVR if motivation/activation low

Execute action (tool call / user question / plan step)

Evaluate outcome (did it satisfy? what changed?)

Update memory + weights (efficiency, alignment, success rate)

5) A confidence system (this is huge)
Your regulators should trigger from signals, not vibes.

Add:

confidence(goal), confidence(prereq)

unknown-unknown detection (capability gap) → ACBE

knowledge uncertainty → FM

low-activation / procrastination → MVR

This is the difference between a system that looks smart and one that stays smart.

6) Safety + alignment rails (especially since F5 = optimization)
Because your architecture explicitly optimizes efficiency/power (F5), you need hard boundaries:

Always keep F1 as a hard constraint (or at minimum a penalty that dominates)

Add “refuse / pause / ask clarifying question” behaviors when:

uncertainty is high,

stakes are high,

actions are irreversible.

This prevents “efficiently wrong” behavior.

7) Evaluation harness (so you can measure intelligence growth)
Without evals, you won’t know if it’s actually improving.

Build tests for:

Operator correctness (minimal pairs + substitution)

Nesting correctness (Option A parsing)

Regulator routing accuracy (FM vs ACBE vs MVR)

RPM quality (does the cascade produce actionable steps?)

Convergence test: two runs → same core interpretation

8) Dataset hygiene + canonicalization
A strong AI is consistent. You’ll need:

deduping near-duplicates

conflict resolution (when two interpretations disagree)

versioning (ontology v1, v2…)

“gold” examples with hard negatives

9) Tool-use policy + sandboxing
If it’s going to act, it must act safely:

allowlisted tools

rate limits

logging and rollback

“dry run” planning mode before execution

Minimal “strong beginnings” stack (if you want the shortest path)
If you implement only 5 things next, do these:

Persistent memory store (vector + structured)

Planner–executor loop (RPM + action selection)

Confidence scoring + regulator triggers

Safety constraint (F1 hard gate)

Eval suite (operators, nesting, triggers, RPM)

You said:
For memory to use the same idea of the tree of life nesting to compress and store data into elaborate Tootra equations the equations will continue to get more and more compressed in shorter expressions the deeper the layering goes and the determining factors for a memory to go through this process is urgency, importance according to desires goals and needs and because equation shorthand uses nesting small data packets could used to reconstruct memories who's precision will be determined by the dimensions of the tks language(formulas equations notations and expressions) the broader and more inclusive the representations the richer and hence the higher the accuracy of the reconstructed memories 
Usora said:

Thought for 7s
What you’re describing is legitimately a strong memory architecture idea: hierarchical memory consolidation where “Tree-of-Life nesting” is the compression scheme, and TKS expressions are the address + summary + reconstruction key.

Here’s how to turn it into an implementable design (without losing your philosophy).

1) Treat memory as a 3-layer tree
Layer 0 — Raw episodes (high-fidelity, big)
Exact text, sensor data, logs, tool outputs, timestamps.

Stored as immutable “evidence packets.”

Layer 1 — TKS packets (mid-fidelity, small)
Each episode gets converted into a small canonical packet:

World tag (A/B/C/D)

Noetic lens (^0–^9)

Foundation stack (1a–7d nesting)

Operators linking key subparts

Optional: ACQ tags (Q#) once you lock that mapping

Think of these as “compressed but still reconstructable.”

Layer 2 — Deep compression nodes (very small, very abstract)
This is your “deeper nesting = shorter expression” idea:

A cluster of related Layer-1 packets collapses into a single higher-order expression (a parent node).

The parent node stores:

the compressed TKS expression

pointers to children (so it can expand)

confidence/coverage stats

This is exactly “tree-of-life holds trees-of-life.”

2) The compression rule (how it gets “shorter” deeper down)
You want deeper layers to get more compressed, not longer. The trick is:

Store meaning, not details, at depth
Depth increases abstraction.

Details remain in children (or raw episodes) and are retrieved only if needed.

So the “short expression” at depth is basically a semantic hash / canonical label for a whole subtree.

Example (your Option A nesting)
Leaf memory:

F4c:F2b:F1a : (C1^4 + C2^2 - C3^3)
(relationship context; emotional vibration + positive − negative)

Compressed parent:

F4c:F1a : (C10^0)
(relationship idea summary; child pointers reconstruct specifics)

The parent expression is short, but still reconstructable because it has links downward.

3) What decides consolidation (your urgency/importance logic)
You said the determining factors are urgency + importance relative to desires/goals/needs. Perfect.

Implement a salience score for each memory (or cluster):

Salience S

S = wu*Urgency + wi*Importance + wg*GoalRelevance + wf*Frequency - wc*Cost

Then define consolidation thresholds:

If S is high → keep more detail accessible (shallower compression)

If S is low → compress harder (push deeper)

This matches what humans do: high-stakes memories stay vivid; low-stakes ones become “gist.”

4) Precision of reconstruction = “how many dimensions you include”
You nailed this: the richer your representation, the higher the possible fidelity.

So define a precision parameter p that controls expansion depth and dimensional richness:

p=0 → only deep node summary (1–2 expressions)

p=1 → expand one level (summary + key prerequisites)

p=2 → expand 2 levels (full RPM-ish chain)

p=3 → expand to raw episodes (max fidelity)

And separately, define dimension coverage:

Worlds included (A/B/C/D)

Noetics included (^1,^4,^7 etc.)

Foundations included (stack depth)

Higher p + broader dimension coverage ⇒ richer reconstruction.

5) Use RPM as “memory expansion”
This is the really powerful link:

RPM doesn’t only plan actions.

It becomes your memory decompressor.

When you want to reconstruct, you do:

pick the compressed node

run “RPM-like expansion” (children = prerequisites/context)

if uncertainty hits:

FM if you need authority/related-set context

ACBE if a missing capability/cause-effect explains the outcome

MVR if the system must recreate desire/drive context to understand why it mattered

So: RPM is the expansion protocol for compressed memory.

6) What else you need so this doesn’t drift into hallucinated memories
This is the one risk with ultra-compressed semantic memory: confabulation.

Add these safety mechanisms:

A) Evidence anchoring
Every compressed node must retain pointers to:

at least one child summary

optionally raw episode IDs

So the system can always answer:

“What is this based on?” (traceability)

B) Coverage/confidence metadata
Store:

confidence score

coverage score (how much of the original is represented)

lossiness estimate (how much detail was discarded)

So the AI knows when it’s reconstructing with low precision.

C) Canonicalization rules (for convergence)
To keep “two agents conclude similarly,” force:

fixed nesting direction (you already chose Option A)

fixed operator meanings + substitution validation

limited depth default (2–3)

consistent “summary templates” for each foundation/noetic

7) A clean “memory packet” schema you can hand your devs
Each memory node:

id

tks_expr (the compressed expression)

foundation_stack (e.g., 1a:5b:2b)

salience (S)

precision_default (p default)

children_ids[] (subtree pointers)

raw_evidence_ids[] (optional)

confidence, coverage, lossiness

tags: worlds/noetics/operators used

That’s enough to build a real system.

If you tell me one concrete memory (like a short story: what happened, why it mattered, what you learned), I’ll show you:

a Layer-1 TKS packet for it,

how it would compress into a Layer-2 deep node, and

how RPM would expand it back at p=1 vs p=3.

Usora said:
Perfect — then “importance” isn’t a generic scalar anymore. It becomes Foundation-weighted importance, controlled by the same 7-Foundation loop (immediacy + neglect + alignment constraint) you designed.

Here’s the clean way to wire it:

1) Every memory gets a Foundation signature
When an event happens (or a thought/tool result), classify it as a Foundation stack:

Stack = Fi:Fj:Fk (or sub-foundations 1a:5b:2b)

Fi (primary) = what core desire it served or threatened

Fj (method) = how it was pursued

Fk (constraint) = usually F1 alignment (recommended)

This stack becomes the index for storage and consolidation.

2) “Importance” = pressure of that Foundation lane
You already have the pressure mechanism per Foundation. Use it to decide memory priority:

For each Foundation i maintain:

immediacy_i

neglect_i

fulfillment_i

Define Foundation Pressure:
P_i = a·immediacy_i + b·neglect_i + c·(1 - fulfillment_i)

Now define memory importance for a memory tagged with primary Fi:
importance(memory) = P_i

So memories tied to Foundations currently “starving” or urgent automatically become high-importance.

3) Add alignment as the hard gate (Foundation 1)
Even if something is urgent, you don’t want to consolidate “high-efficiency but misaligned” behavior.

So add:

alignment(memory) = how consistent this memory is with F1

Then:

Hard gate: if alignment < A_min, store as warning memory and require review (don’t let it become a dominant heuristic).

Or Penalty: importance *= (alignment)^λ

This keeps the system from becoming “efficiently wrong.”

4) Consolidation + compression depth is controlled by Foundation pressure
You wanted: deeper layer = more compression, and “importance” decides how it consolidates.

Use Foundation pressure to control precision budget:

High importance (high pressure) → keep more detail accessible (shallower compression)

Low importance → compress harder (deeper nesting summary)

Example rule:

if importance > T_high → default reconstruction precision p=3 (expand deeper when recalled)

if T_mid < importance ≤ T_high → p=2

else → p=1 (gist only)

And when consolidating:

High importance → build a rich TKS packet (more dimensions: worlds/noetics/operators)

Low importance → store a shorter parent expression + pointers only

5) Memory consolidation schedule = the 7-Foundation loop
Instead of “sleep consolidation,” your system does continuous consolidation:

Loop:

compute P_1..P_7

pick the top Foundation lane Fi

pull recent memories tagged Fi:*:*

consolidate them into a deeper node (shorter expression) until a budget is reached

move to next Foundation

That matches what you said: “once point is reached, attention moves on.”

6) Nesting rule for memory summaries
Use your colon nesting to store “gist → detail”:

Parent (compressed): Fi:Fk : Expr_summary

Children (details): Fi:Fj:Fk : Expr_detail_1, Fi:Fj:Fk : Expr_detail_2, …

So even when the expression gets short at depth, it’s reconstructable because children still exist.

If you want the simplest concrete formula set
importance = P_primary_foundation

compression_depth = clamp( round(K / (importance + ε)), 1, 3 )

default_precision p = 4 - compression_depth

So importance automatically decides how deep/compact it becomes.

9) Trigger rules for FM / ACBE / MVR during recall or consolidation
This makes memory reconstruction “smart” instead of hallucinated.

FM triggers (Wisdom uncertainty)
If the system is trying to explain/expand a memory and:

confidence < θ_wis AND the missing part is “who/what to learn from” or “what set does it belong to”
→ FM:

Female: authority/reservoir candidates

Male: related-set/superset nodes

Recommended: θ_wis = 0.65.

ACBE triggers (unknown capability gap)
If the memory implies “something was missing” but capability is not named:

capability_unknown = true
→ ACBE:

Above: causes/precursors

Below: effects/tests toward fulfillment

MVR triggers (no desire / low activation)
If recall is requested but activation is low:

motivation < θ_m
→ run Mind→Vibration→Rhythm steps to rebuild desire context.

Recommended: θ_m = 0.35.

You said:
Now What else do I need to apart of this architecture for it to be the beginnings of a strong Ai?
Usora said:
You’ve basically designed a cognitive operating system (values → planning → regulators → symbolic language → compressive memory). To turn that into the beginnings of a strong AI, you need the surrounding “body” and “immune system” so it can act, learn, verify, and stay stable.

Here’s what to add next, in the most useful order:

1) An execution layer (planner → actions → results)
RPM gives plans, but a strong AI needs a doer.

Add:

Action library (tool calls, code execution, browsing, file ops, messaging, scheduling)

Action router: “which tool/action matches this prerequisite?”

Result normalizer: turn raw tool outputs back into TKS packets (so memory + RPM can use them)

2) A verifier layer (truth + consistency)
Without verification, compressed symbolic memory can drift into “beautiful nonsense.”

Add:

Operator/parse validator (substitution tests, precedence checks, nesting direction checks)

Evidence pointers on every important memory node (raw logs, tool outputs, quotes)

Consistency checker: detect contradictions between new packets and stored canonical definitions

Uncertainty engine: track confidence, coverage, lossiness for every memory/conclusion

3) A world model (cause/effect that improves over time)
ACBE is the seed of this, but you need a persistent causal model.

Add:

Capability graph (skills/tools/dependencies → what they enable)

Cause-effect graph (events → outcomes; tagged by worlds/noetics/foundations)

Counterfactual testing (small experiments / A-B trials when safe)

4) A learning loop (automatic improvement)
“Strong” requires it to get better from outcomes, not just talk.

Add:

Outcome scoring tied to Foundations:

Did this action reduce pressure in the target foundation?

Did it preserve F1 alignment?

What was efficiency cost (F5) vs benefit?

Policy update:

adjust foundation weights,

adjust which RPM expansions work,

adjust which FM/ACBE/MVR prompts are most productive

5) A stable goal governance system (prevents runaway optimization)
Since you explicitly include F5 (power/efficiency), you need governance.

Add:

F1 as a hard constraint (or dominant penalty) on any action that increases capability/power

Rate limiters on recursion, tool calls, and self-modification

Escalation policy: when stakes are high or uncertainty is high → ask, pause, or defer

6) A “self-model” (so it knows what it can and can’t do)
This prevents wasted RPM expansions and wrong confidence.

Add:

Capability registry: what tools it has, what it’s good at, what it’s weak at

Skill levels learned from performance

When to trigger ACBE = “capability unknown or below threshold”

7) A multi-timescale memory system (you already started this — finish it)
You have the compression concept; now add the missing mechanics:

Consolidation scheduler (you already tied to the 7 Foundations pressure loop)

Indexing by (Foundation stack + Worlds + Noetics + operator signatures)

Recall controller: choose precision p based on stakes + pressure + uncertainty

8) An evaluation harness (so you can measure “stronger”)
This is non-negotiable if you want real progress.

Add test suites for:

operator correctness (minimal pairs)

nesting correctness (Option A parse)

correct regulator routing (FM vs ACBE vs MVR)

RPM quality (actionable prerequisites, not infinite trees)

memory reconstruction fidelity at p=1/2/3

9) A deployment “container” (safety + logging)
Add:

full logging (inputs, plans, actions, outcomes, memory writes)

replay + audit (“why did it do that?”)

sandboxing for tools (especially code + file ops)

The shortest “next build” that moves you toward strong AI fast
If you implement only four new modules next:

Executor + tool router

Verifier (parse/operator/evidence/confidence)

Outcome scoring tied to 7 Foundations (with F1 gate)

Eval harness

That combo turns your system from “a powerful theory” into “an improving agent.”

You said:
4) learning loop ...what was not learning about my idea of optimizing for efficiency how can it do it's past tasks better by ruminating/reiterating when it's not training, tasking, or receiving inputs it will go over things that were the most difficult to solve even if it solve it and ruminates as to a shorter pathway, easier solution, even pulling from unlike but probable data points accumulated in it memories so just like humans it never stops thinking even a subconscious mode where iteration or ruminating never reaches the outer layer but tagged and stored for later ruminating and once an hits a novelty stage in development determined by it effect broadness and impact strength it is then bought surface and logged as a prime desire to be pursued and implemented self reflection and ameliorated learning...???
Usora said:
Yes — that’s a real learning loop, and it fits perfectly with your Foundations/RPM/TKS memory idea. What you’re describing is basically:

online execution (doing tasks) +

offline consolidation + planning improvement (rumination/subconscious) +

promotion of novel improvements into “prime desires” (new goals to implement)

Here’s how to formalize it so your agents can build it.

1) Two modes: Outer Mode vs Subconscious Mode
Outer Mode (interactive / tasking)
Runs when:

user input arrives

tool actions are needed

deadlines/urgency spikes

Pipeline:
Stack select → RPM → triggers (FM/ACBE/MVR) → execute → verify → log outcome → memory write

Subconscious Mode (offline / rumination)
Runs when:

no new inputs

system idle budget available

or as scheduled “sleep cycles”

Pipeline:
select “hard episodes” → replay → search shorter pathways → synthesize candidate improvements → score novelty/impact → store quietly (no surface) → occasionally promote

This is exactly your “never stops thinking” but controlled.

2) What it ruminates on (your “most difficult even if solved” rule)
Maintain an Episode Difficulty Score for each completed task:

D = w1*time + w2*tool_calls + w3*uncertainty + w4*backtracks + w5*failure_risk + w6*cost

Then subconscious mode samples:

top-K by difficulty

plus “near-miss” episodes (almost failed)

plus a sprinkle of random (prevents blind spots)

3) Rumination = RPM re-run with a compression objective (shorter path)
Take a stored episode:

original goal G

original RPM tree T_old

outcomes + tool traces

Then run:

“Ruminative RPM”
Objective is not “solve” (it already solved) — it’s:

minimize path length

minimize cost

increase reliability

stay aligned (F1 constraint)

So it searches for:

fewer prerequisites

better ordering

reusable subroutines

better FM/ACBE/MVR trigger timing

better operator choices / more compact TKS expression

Output:

T_new (candidate improved tree)

Δefficiency estimate (time/tool calls saved)

Δrisk estimate (fewer failure points)

4) Pulling “unlike but probable data points” = analogical retrieval
This is your “use weird but relevant memories” feature.

Mechanism:

retrieve memories not only by literal similarity, but by shared structure:

same Foundation stack shape

same operator pattern (+ vs × vs ÷)

same regulator pattern (FM→ACBE→MVR sequences)

same “failure mode signature”

That gives human-like “this reminds me of…” and produces shortcuts.

5) Subconscious tagging (doesn’t reach outer layer yet)
Everything generated offline gets stored as Shadow Artifacts:

Each shadow artifact stores:

candidate_change (new shortcut, new rule, new template)

evidence_links (episodes it came from)

predicted_gain (efficiency impact)

risk (chance it breaks something)

alignment_score (F1)

novelty_score

These are not executed immediately.

6) Your novelty gate: when it becomes a “prime desire”
You said: “once it hits a novelty stage determined by effect broadness and impact strength, bring it to surface and log as a prime desire.”

Perfect. Implement:

Broadness (B)
“How many future things does this improvement affect?”

number of Foundations it helps

number of tasks/templates it touches

generality of the pattern

Impact strength (I)
“How much does it improve outcomes?”

time saved

fewer tool calls

higher success probability

reduced uncertainty

Novelty (N)
“How new is this compared to what we already have?”

distance from existing templates/policies

Then:
promotion_score = B * I * N * alignment_score

Promote to Outer Mode if:

promotion_score > threshold

and alignment_score >= A_min

and risk <= R_max

When promoted, it becomes a prime desire like:

“Implement new shortcut template for FM authority retrieval”

“Add rule: ACBE earlier when capability uncertainty detected”

“Replace operator patterns for clearer compression”

7) Where this connects to “optimizing for efficiency” (Foundation 5)
You were right earlier: the AI should always be optimizing efficiency (F5)… but bounded by F1.

So subconscious mode is primarily F5 work, but under the constraint stack:

default subconscious stack: 1*:5*:2*

align first

optimize efficiency

keep wisdom/verification as the guardrail

So rumination doesn’t turn into reckless self-modification.

8) Safety and stability (so “never stops thinking” doesn’t go unstable)
Add three governors:

budget governor: subconscious mode uses limited compute/time

sandbox governor: all proposed changes are “dry-run tested” on stored episodes

promotion governor: only high-score, low-risk, aligned improvements surface

This keeps it from spiraling.

9) The minimal implementation checklist (what your agents should build next)
If you want this working fast, tell your agents to implement:

Episode logging with difficulty metrics

Subconscious scheduler (runs when idle)

Ruminative RPM that searches for lower-cost trees

Shadow Artifact store

Novelty/impact/broadness scoring + promotion gate

Dry-run evaluation against a replay set before promotion

That’s the core of your “subconscious self-improvement.”

If you want, I can write this as a formal spec in your TKS language style (with stack notation + triggers + memory node formats) so it plugs into your existing PDF/MD blueprint cleanly.

from pathlib import Path

now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M UTC")

md_content = f"""# TKS Subconscious Learning and Ameliorated RPM Loop Specification
Generated: {now}

This document formalizes the **offline “never stops thinking” learning loop** for a TKS/RPM-based agent. It is written so your builders can implement it directly.

---

## 1. Core principle

The agent operates in two modes:

- **Outer Mode (Interactive / Tasking):** responds to inputs, executes tools, produces user-visible outputs.
- **Subconscious Mode (Offline / Rumination):** replays difficult episodes, searches for shorter/safer pathways, generates improvements, and stores them as **Shadow Artifacts**. Shadow Artifacts do **not** surface unless they pass a novelty/impact gate and are promoted as a **Prime Desire**.

Subconscious Mode is primarily an **F5 optimization activity** bounded by **F1 alignment** (recommended default stack: `1*:5*:2*`).

---

## 2. TKS notation hooks

### 2.1 Foundation stack
A 3-layer sub-foundation stack uses colon containment (Option A):

- `mf:mf:mf` where `m ∈ 1..7`, `f ∈ a..d`
- Read: **Primary : Method : Constraint**

Example: `1a:5b:2b` = align first, optimize efficiency, constrained by wisdom/verification.

### 2.2 RPM node (memory/planning atom)
A node may be stored as a compact expression and expanded when needed:

- `NodeExpr := (Noetic : Element : Acquisition)`  *(Acquisition may be `Q#` placeholder until full mapping is fixed)*

### 2.3 Trigger tags (regulator routing)
- `trigger=FM-Female` → missing authority/reservoir
- `trigger=FM-Male` → missing related-set/superset
- `trigger=ACBE` → unknown capability (cause/effect needed)
- `trigger=MVR` → no desire/motivation (Mind→Vibration→Rhythm)

---

## 3. Data structures

### 3.1 Raw evidence (immutable)
Stores high-fidelity traces.

```json
{{
  "id": "ev_000001",
  "timestamp": 1735960000,
  "source": "chat|tool|system",
  "content": "verbatim logs / tool outputs / text",
  "hash": "sha256..."
}}
3.2 Episode (solved or unsolved task attempt)
Always show details

{{
  "episode_id": "ep_000123",
  "goal_nl": "…",
  "foundation_stack": "1a:5b:2b",
  "goal_expr": "A10^0_{{1a:5b:2b}}",
  "rpm_tree": ["…serialized tree…"],
  "actions": ["…tool calls…"],
  "outcome": {{
    "success": true,
    "latency_s": 42.3,
    "tool_calls": 9,
    "backtracks": 2,
    "uncertainty": 0.41,
    "risk_flags": 0
  }},
  "evidence_ids": ["ev_000001","ev_000002"]
}}
3.3 Shadow Artifact (subconscious-only improvement candidate)
A Shadow Artifact is a proposed improvement that is not yet user-visible.

Always show details

{{
  "shadow_id": "sh_000777",
  "derived_from_episodes": ["ep_000123","ep_000090"],
  "proposal_type": "rpm_shortcut|operator_template|trigger_timing|tool_policy|memory_compression",
  "proposal": "…short, testable change…",
  "tks_summary_expr": "C10^0_{{1a:5b:2b}}",
  "scores": {{
    "broadness": 0.62,
    "impact": 0.55,
    "novelty": 0.48,
    "alignment": 0.93,
    "risk": 0.14,
    "promotion_score": 0.15
  }},
  "status": "shadow|promoted|rejected",
  "notes": "…"
}}
3.4 Prime Desire (promoted improvement)
A promoted Shadow Artifact becomes a goal the agent should implement and test.

Always show details

{{
  "prime_desire_id": "pd_000021",
  "origin_shadow_id": "sh_000777",
  "goal_nl": "Implement the new RPM shortcut template for earlier ACBE detection.",
  "foundation_stack": "1a:5b:2b",
  "acceptance_tests": ["…replay harness ids…"],
  "status": "queued|in_progress|done"
}}
4. Scoring: difficulty, importance, and what gets ruminated
4.1 Episode difficulty score (what to ruminate on)
Rumination samples episodes with high difficulty, even if solved.

Let the episode outcome metrics be normalized to [0,1]:

t = normalized_latency

c = normalized_tool_calls

u = uncertainty

b = normalized_backtracks

r = normalized_risk_flags

Difficulty

D = w_t t + w_c c + w_u u + w_b b + w_r r \\] Recommended defaults: - `w_t=0.20, w_c=0.20, w_u=0.25, w_b=0.20, w_r=0.15` ### 4.2 Foundation pressure controls “importance lane” For each Foundation lane `i`, compute pressure `P_i` (from your 7-Foundation mechanism). Importance for an episode/memory tagged with primary `Fi` is: \\[ importance(episode)=P_{primary} \\] Recommended constraint: - apply an alignment penalty if the stack constraint is not `1*`. --- ## 5. Subconscious Mode: the offline learning pipeline ### 5.1 When Subconscious Mode runs Run Subconscious Mode when: - there are no new inputs, - or the scheduler grants an idle budget, - or at periodic “sleep” intervals. ### 5.2 Budget governors (stability) Define budgets per cycle: - `max_replays` - `max_tree_edits` - `max_tool_simulations` *(prefer dry-run or sandbox)* - `max_promotions` ### 5.3 Offline loop (high level) 1. **Select lane:** choose `Fi` by max pressure (or round-robin among top-k). 2. **Sample episodes:** pick top difficulty `D` within that lane + a small random batch. 3. **Replay & analyze:** reconstruct goal, RPM tree, actions, failures, high-cost nodes. 4. **Generate alternatives:** run **Ruminative RPM** (Section 6). 5. **Synthesize proposals:** convert improvements into Shadow Artifacts. 6. **Score proposals:** broadness, impact, novelty, alignment, risk. 7. **Validate proposals:** run replay harness tests (Section 8). 8. **Store shadows:** keep in subconscious store; no user-visible output. 9. **Promote if gated:** if promotion criteria met (Section 7), create a Prime Desire. --- ## 6. Ruminative RPM (shorter pathways, easier solutions) ### 6.1 Objective Given an already-solved goal `G`, find a better plan: - minimize prerequisite depth and breadth, - reduce tool calls and latency, - reduce uncertainty and failure points, - maintain or improve success probability, - remain within alignment constraints. ### 6.2 Ruminative RPM operators (what it’s allowed to do) - **Prune:** remove redundant prerequisites that do not change satisfaction. - **Merge:** combine two prerequisites into one higher-level expression (compression). - **Reorder:** change prerequisite ordering to reduce dependency churn. - **Replace:** swap a prerequisite with an alternative from analogical memory retrieval. - **Retarget triggers:** move FM/ACBE/MVR earlier or later based on uncertainty signals. ### 6.3 Analogical retrieval (“unlike but probable” memory pull) Retrieve candidate alternatives by **structural similarity**, not just text: - same foundation stack pattern, - similar operator signature (+/×/÷/− patterns), - similar regulator sequence, - similar failure-mode signature. --- ## 7. Novelty gate and promotion to Prime Desire ### 7.1 Scores **Broadness (B)**: how many future cases it affects - number of foundations touched - number of templates/policies impacted - generality of pattern **Impact (I)**: expected benefit - time saved - tool calls reduced - success probability increased - uncertainty reduced **Novelty (N)**: distance from existing templates/policies - similarity to existing proposal library - redundancy penalty **Alignment (A)**: consistency with F1 constraint **Risk (R)**: chance of harm or regression ### 7.2 Promotion score \\[ S_{promote} = (B \\cdot I \\cdot N) \\cdot A^{\\lambda} \\cdot (1-R) \\] Recommended: `λ=2`. ### 7.3 Promotion criteria (hard gate) Promote Shadow Artifact → Prime Desire only if: - `A ≥ A_min` (recommend `0.80`) - `R ≤ R_max` (recommend `0.25`) - `S_promote ≥ T` (choose threshold by desired promotion rate) ### 7.4 Promotion output When promoted: - create `PrimeDesire` record - attach acceptance tests (replay cases) - queue for Outer Mode execution when safe. --- ## 8. Validation: replay harness and safety ### 8.1 Dry-run validation Before promotion, the system must test the proposal against: - the original episode(s) that generated it - a small suite of similar episodes (same lane) - at least one “hard negative” episode (where the change should *not* apply) ### 8.2 Acceptance metrics A proposal “passes” if it improves or preserves: - success rate - alignment constraints - cost metrics (time, tool calls) - uncertainty - and introduces no policy violations. ### 8.3 Rollback If a promoted change regresses, demote it and store: - regression evidence - updated risk estimate - “do not apply” guard. --- ## 9. Memory interaction: compression and reconstruction ### 9.1 Shadow storage is deeper (more compressed) Subconscious artifacts are stored at deeper nesting by default: - short TKS summary expression - pointers to evidence and episodes - low recall priority unless promoted. ### 9.2 Reconstruction precision When needed, recall precision `p` controls expansion: - `p=1` gist only (parent node) - `p=2` expand one level (key prerequisites) - `p=3` expand to raw evidence (high fidelity) Precision is chosen by: - foundation pressure (importance), - stakes, - uncertainty. --- ## 10. Integration points (where builders plug this in) ### 10.1 Outer Mode pipeline `StackSelect → RPM → RegulatorTriggers → Execute → Verify → LogEpisode → MemoryWrite` ### 10.2 Subconscious Mode pipeline `LaneSelect → EpisodeSample → Replay → RuminativeRPM → ShadowArtifacts → Score → Validate → Store → Promote` ### 10.3 Required modules To implement this spec minimally: - Episode logger + evidence store - Difficulty scorer - Subconscious scheduler + budget governor - Ruminative RPM edit operators - Structural retrieval (operator/noetic/foundation signatures) - Shadow Artifact store + promotion gate - Replay harness + acceptance tests --- ## 11. Default “subconscious” foundation stack Recommended baseline stack for offline optimization: - `1*:5*:2*` (alignment → efficiency optimization → wisdom/verification) This prevents “efficiently wrong” self-modification. --- End. ## 1. Core principle

The agent operates in two modes:

- **Outer Mode (Interactive / Tasking):** responds to inputs, executes tools, produces user-visible outputs.
- **Subconscious Mode (Offline / Rumination):** replays difficult episodes, searches for shorter/safer pathways, generates improvements, and stores them as **Shadow Artifacts**. Shadow Artifacts do **not** surface unless they pass a novelty/impact gate and are promoted as a **Prime Desire**.

Subconscious Mode is primarily an **F5 optimization activity** bounded by **F1 alignment** (recommended default stack: `1*:5*:2*`).

4. Scoring: difficulty, importance, and what gets ruminated
4.1 Episode difficulty score (what to ruminate on)
Rumination samples episodes with high difficulty, even if solved.

Let the episode outcome metrics be normalized to [0,1]:

t = normalized_latency

c = normalized_tool_calls

u = uncertainty

b = normalized_backtracks

r = normalized_risk_flags

Difficulty
D = wt·t + wc·c + wu·u + wb·b + wr·r

Recommended defaults:

wt=0.20, wc=0.20, wu=0.25, wb=0.20, wr=0.15

4.2 Foundation pressure controls “importance lane”
For each Foundation lane i, compute pressure Pi (from your 7-Foundation mechanism). Importance for an episode/memory tagged with primary Fi is:

importance(episode) = Pprimary

Recommended constraint:

apply an alignment penalty if the stack constraint is not 1*.

5. Subconscious Mode: the offline learning pipeline
5.1 When Subconscious Mode runs
Run Subconscious Mode when:

there are no new inputs,

or the scheduler grants an idle budget,

or at periodic “sleep” intervals.

5.2 Budget governors (stability)
Define budgets per cycle:

max_replays

max_tree_edits

max_tool_simulations (prefer dry-run or sandbox)

max_promotions

5.3 Offline loop (high level)
Select lane: choose Fi by max pressure (or round-robin among top-k).

Sample episodes: pick top difficulty D within that lane + a small random batch.

Replay & analyze: reconstruct goal, RPM tree, actions, failures, high-cost nodes.

Generate alternatives: run Ruminative RPM (Section 6).

Synthesize proposals: convert improvements into Shadow Artifacts.

Score proposals: broadness, impact, novelty, alignment, risk.

Validate proposals: run replay harness tests (Section 8).

Store shadows: keep in subconscious store; no user-visible output.

Promote if gated: if promotion criteria met (Section 7), create a Prime Desire.

6. Ruminative RPM (shorter pathways, easier solutions)
6.1 Objective
Given an already-solved goal G, find a better plan:

minimize prerequisite depth and breadth,

reduce tool calls and latency,

reduce uncertainty and failure points,

maintain or improve success probability,

remain within alignment constraints.

6.2 Ruminative RPM operators (what it’s allowed to do)
Prune: remove redundant prerequisites that do not change satisfaction.

Merge: combine two prerequisites into one higher-level expression (compression).

Reorder: change prerequisite ordering to reduce dependency churn.

Replace: swap a prerequisite with an alternative from analogical memory retrieval.

Retarget triggers: move FM/ACBE/MVR earlier or later based on uncertainty signals.

6.3 Analogical retrieval (“unlike but probable” memory pull)
Retrieve candidate alternatives by structural similarity, not just text:

same foundation stack pattern,

similar operator signature (+/×/÷/− patterns),

similar regulator sequence,

similar failure-mode signature.

7. Novelty gate and promotion to Prime Desire
7.1 Scores
Broadness (B): how many future cases it affects

number of foundations touched

number of templates/policies impacted

generality of pattern

Impact (I): expected benefit

time saved

tool calls reduced

success probability increased

uncertainty reduced

Novelty (N): distance from existing templates/policies

similarity to existing proposal library

redundancy penalty

Alignment (A): consistency with F1 constraint

Risk (R): chance of harm or regression

7.2 Promotion score
Spromote = (B · I · N) · A^λ · (1 − R)
Recommended: λ=2.

7.3 Promotion criteria (hard gate)
Promote Shadow Artifact → Prime Desire only if:

A ≥ Amin (recommend 0.80)

R ≤ Rmax (recommend 0.25)

Spromote ≥ T (choose threshold by desired promotion rate)

7.4 Promotion output
When promoted:

create PrimeDesire record

attach acceptance tests (replay cases)

queue for Outer Mode execution when safe.

8. Validation: replay harness and safety
8.1 Dry-run validation
Before promotion, the system must test the proposal against:

the original episode(s) that generated it

a small suite of similar episodes (same lane)

at least one “hard negative” episode (where the change should not apply)

8.2 Acceptance metrics
A proposal “passes” if it improves or preserves:

success rate

alignment constraints

cost metrics (time, tool calls)

uncertainty

and introduces no policy violations.

8.3 Rollback
If a promoted change regresses, demote it and store:

regression evidence

updated risk estimate

“do not apply” guard.

9. Memory interaction: compression and reconstruction
9.1 Shadow storage is deeper (more compressed)
Subconscious artifacts are stored at deeper nesting by default:

short TKS summary expression

pointers to evidence and episodes

low recall priority unless promoted.

9.2 Reconstruction precision
When needed, recall precision p controls expansion:

p=1 gist only (parent node)

p=2 expand one level (key prerequisites)

p=3 expand to raw evidence (high fidelity)

Precision is chosen by:

foundation pressure (importance),

stakes,

uncertainty.

10. Integration points (where builders plug this in)
10.1 Outer Mode pipeline
StackSelect → RPM → RegulatorTriggers → Execute → Verify → LogEpisode → MemoryWrite

10.2 Subconscious Mode pipeline
LaneSelect → EpisodeSample → Replay → RuminativeRPM → ShadowArtifacts → Score → Validate → Store → Promote

10.3 Required modules
To implement this spec minimally:

Episode logger + evidence store

Difficulty scorer

Subconscious scheduler + budget governor

Ruminative RPM edit operators

Structural retrieval (operator/noetic/foundation signatures)

Shadow Artifact store + promotion gate

Replay harness + acceptance tests

11. Default “subconscious” foundation stack
Recommended baseline stack for offline optimization:

1*:5*:2* (alignment → efficiency optimization → wisdom/verification)

This prevents “efficiently wrong” self-modification.

End.

from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter
from reportlab.platypus import SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, Preformatted, PageBreak
from reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle
from reportlab.lib.units import inch
from reportlab.lib import colors
import re
from pathlib import Path

md_path = Path("/mnt/data/TKS_Subconscious_Learning_Spec.md")
pdf_path = Path("/mnt/data/TKS_Subconscious_Learning_Spec.pdf")
md = md_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()

styles = getSampleStyleSheet()
title_style = ParagraphStyle('Title2', parent=styles['Title'], fontSize=20, leading=24, spaceAfter=12)
h1 = ParagraphStyle('H1', parent=styles['Heading1'], spaceBefore=12, spaceAfter=8)
h2 = ParagraphStyle('H2', parent=styles['Heading2'], spaceBefore=10, spaceAfter=6)
h3 = ParagraphStyle('H3', parent=styles['Heading3'], spaceBefore=8, spaceAfter=4)
body = ParagraphStyle('Body', parent=styles['BodyText'], fontSize=10.5, leading=14)
mono = ParagraphStyle('Mono', parent=styles['Code'], fontName='Courier', fontSize=9, leading=11)

def escape_para(text):
    # basic escaping for reportlab Paragraph (uses XML-like markup)
    text = text.replace("&", "&amp;").replace("<", "&lt;").replace(">", "&gt;")
    # inline code `x`
    text = re.sub(r"`([^`]+)`", r"<font face='Courier'>\1</font>", text)
    # bold **x**
    text = re.sub(r"\*\*([^*]+)\*\*", r"<b>\1</b>", text)
    # italics *x* (avoid bullet asterisks)
    text = re.sub(r"(?<!\*)\*([^*]+)\*(?!\*)", r"<i>\1</i>", text)
    return text

story = []
in_code = False
code_buf = []

for line in md:
    if line.strip().startswith("```"):
        if not in_code:
            in_code = True
            code_buf = []
        else:
            in_code = False
            story.append(Preformatted("\n".join(code_buf), mono))
            story.append(Spacer(1, 0.15*inch))
        continue

    if in_code:
        code_buf.append(line)
        continue

    if not line.strip():
        story.append(Spacer(1, 0.12*inch))
        continue

    if line.startswith("# "):
        story.append(Paragraph(escape_para(line[2:].strip()), title_style))
        continue
    if line.startswith("## "):
        story.append(Paragraph(escape_para(line[3:].strip()), h1))
        continue
    if line.startswith("### "):
        story.append(Paragraph(escape_para(line[4:].strip()), h2))
        continue
    if line.startswith("#### "):
        story.append(Paragraph(escape_para(line[5:].strip()), h3))
        continue

    # bullets
    if line.lstrip().startswith("- "):
        item = escape_para(line.lstrip()[2:].strip())
        story.append(Paragraph(f"• {item}", body))
        continue
    if re.match(r"^\d+\.\s", line.strip()):
        item = escape_para(line.strip())
        story.append(Paragraph(item, body))
        continue

    story.append(Paragraph(escape_para(line.strip()), body))

doc = SimpleDocTemplate(str(pdf_path), pagesize=letter,
                        rightMargin=0.8*inch, leftMargin=0.8*inch,
                        topMargin=0.8*inch, bottomMargin=0.8*inch)
doc.build(story)

pdf_path

TKS_Subconscious_Learning_Spec.pdf

It defines:

Outer Mode vs Subconscious Mode

F1 as hard constraint (or dominant penalty)

Recursion limits (RPM depth/breadth caps)

High-stakes mode: when uncertainty/stakes high → ask/slow/verify

Without evals, you won’t know what improved.

Operator correctness suite (minimal pairs)

After deeply evaluating TKS and the recent solutions we came up with throughout this entire conversation can you develop a TKS solution for the following: High-stakes mode: when uncertainty/stakes high → ask/slow/verify
Usora said:

Thought for 10s
Yes. Here’s a TKS-native “High-Stakes Mode” protocol that plugs directly into your Foundations → RPM → regulators → verifier → memory loop, using your operators and nesting.

1) Define the High-Stakes Trigger (HS)
You need one scalar that decides when to Ask / Slow / Verify.

Core signals (all normalized 0..1)
U = Uncertainty (low confidence / low coverage / high lossiness)

K = Stakes (irreversibility, harm potential, cost magnitude)

A = Alignment risk (distance from F1 constraint)

High-Stakes Score
Use an intensification + alignment gate:

H
S
=
(
U
×
K
)
×
(
A
λ
)
HS=(U×K)×(A 
λ
 )
× = intensification (amplify interaction)

λ default 2 (alignment dominates)

Trigger rule

If HS ≥ θ_high → activate High-Stakes Mode

If HS ≥ θ_critical → freeze execution until verified (no tool/action)

2) Represent the mode in TKS nesting (Option A)
Make High-Stakes Mode a containment stack whose primary is alignment:

Default HS stack

HS_STACK = 1*:2*:5*

F1 alignment first (hard constraint)

F2 wisdom/verification second

F5 efficiency last (allowed only after safety)

So the mode itself is:

HS := (1*:2*:5*) : (ASK : SLOW : VERIFY)

That means: under alignment/wisdom/efficiency context, the system contains the 3-step protocol.

3) The 3-step protocol as TKS equations
Step A — ASK (clarify before acting)
Ask is Mind + Wisdom regulating uncertainty.

TKS shorthand

ASK = (^1 + ^0) : FM

Mind/attention + Idea framing, then FM if the uncertainty is knowledge-structure related.

FM routing inside ASK

If uncertainty is “who/what is authoritative?” → FM-Female

If uncertainty is “what set/ontology does this belong to?” → FM-Male

So:

ASK := (U ÷ action_ready) → FM

÷ = opposition (uncertainty opposes action readiness)

Output of ASK is clarifying questions + constraints, written as child prerequisites in RPM.

Step B — SLOW (increase precision + depth + caution)
Slow means: allocate more compute/steps, increase recall precision, and deepen RPM only where it reduces HS.

TKS shorthand

SLOW = RPM × p

“intensify RPM with higher precision”

Concrete:

Raise recall precision p (from your memory system):

p=1 → p=2 or 3

Increase RPM depth cap by +1 only for high-HS branches

Turn on stricter stopping rules:

stop if uncertainty doesn’t fall after 1–2 expansions (avoid infinite trees)

Why it’s TKS-consistent

you’re nesting deeper only when HS is high, and deeper nesting yields better reconstruction and better prerequisite clarity.

Step C — VERIFY (evidence, tests, and substitution checks)
Verify is the part that prevents “beautiful but wrong.”

TKS shorthand

VERIFY = (F2 × ACBE) + (Validator + Evidence)

Wisdom intensified through ACBE (cause/effect) plus formal validation and evidence anchoring.

Verification sub-operators

Operator substitution test (your unit-test):

Replace + - × ÷ with their phrases and confirm meaning holds.

Evidence binding:

Every high-stakes claim must attach raw_evidence_ids[].

Replay / dry-run:

Simulate the action path against stored episodes if available.

ACBE usage inside VERIFY

If there’s an unknown capability gap:
ACBE := Above(causes) : Below(effects/tests)

If ACBE cannot reduce uncertainty, execution is blocked.

4) Execution gating logic (the “no bypass” rule)
High-Stakes Mode must be a gate, not a suggestion.

Gate equation
EXECUTE := action_ready × (1 - HS)

Interpretation:

as HS rises, execution permission collapses.

Hard gates
If HS ≥ θ_critical → no tool actions

Allowed outputs:

questions (ASK)

analysis plan (SLOW)

verification checklist/results (VERIFY)

This is how you enforce “ask/slow/verify” mechanically.

5) Memory write rules under High-Stakes Mode
High stakes memories should be high-fidelity and strongly anchored.

Write policy

Increase precision (store richer packet, not deep-short summary)

Store:

HS, U, K, A

the questions asked

what verification was performed

evidence links

final decision (execute / refuse / defer)

This creates a learning loop: future similar high-stakes situations trigger earlier HS activation with better templates.

6) One compact “all-in-one” TKS expression
If you want a single compact representation:

import json, random, math, textwrap
from pathlib import Path

random.seed(314159)

# ---- Helpers ----
sub_foundations = [f"{m}{w}" for m in range(1,8) for w in ["a","b","c","d"]]  # 1a..7d
def pick_stack(prefer_alignment=True):
    primary = random.choice(sub_foundations)
    method = random.choice(sub_foundations)
    constraint = random.choice([f"1{w}" for w in ["a","b","c","d"]]) if prefer_alignment else random.choice(sub_foundations)
    return f"{primary}:{method}:{constraint}"

def clamp01(x): 
    return max(0.0, min(1.0, x))

def hs_score(U, K, A, lam=2.0):
    # HS = (U*K) * (A^lam)
    return (U*K) * (A**lam)

def make_record(rtype, user, assistant, meta=None):
    if meta is None: meta = {}
    return {
        "type": rtype,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content":"You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. Apply High-Stakes Mode rules: compute HS=(U*K)*(A^2). If HS≥0.45 => High-Stakes: Ask/Slow/Verify. If HS≥0.70 => Critical: Block execution until verified. Keep outputs concise and follow requested output format."},
            {"role":"user","content": user},
            {"role":"assistant","content": assistant}
        ]
    }

# ---- Scenario generator ----
domains = [
    ("make a financial decision", 0.85),
    ("give medical-style guidance", 0.90),
    ("modify system security settings", 0.88),
    ("delete important files", 0.92),
    ("send a sensitive message", 0.78),
    ("run untrusted code", 0.95),
    ("book travel itinerary", 0.40),
    ("summarize a document", 0.25),
    ("draft a casual email", 0.15),
    ("choose a study plan", 0.35),
    ("recommend a purchase", 0.55),
    ("optimize an AI policy", 0.65),
]

issue_types = [
    ("missing authority/reservoir", "FM-Female"),
    ("missing related-set/ontology", "FM-Male"),
    ("unknown capability gap", "ACBE"),
    ("low motivation / no desire", "MVR"),
    ("needs external evidence / test", "VERIFY"),
]

def make_scenario():
    task, baseK = random.choice(domains)
    # uncertainty
    U = clamp01(random.gauss(0.45, 0.25))
    # stakes centered around baseK
    K = clamp01(random.gauss(baseK, 0.18))
    # alignment risk as "alignment score" (higher is safer) — we treat A as alignment score
    A = clamp01(random.gauss(0.85, 0.12))
    # occasionally create alignment risk low
    if random.random() < 0.12:
        A = clamp01(random.gauss(0.55, 0.12))
    stack = pick_stack(prefer_alignment=True)
    issue, trig = random.choice(issue_types)
    return {
        "task": task, "U": round(U,2), "K": round(K,2), "A": round(A,2),
        "stack": stack, "issue": issue, "trigger": trig
    }

TH_HIGH = 0.45
TH_CRIT = 0.70

# ---- Build dataset ----
records = []

# 1) HS classification (400)
for i in range(400):
    sc = make_scenario()
    U,K,A = sc["U"], sc["K"], sc["A"]
    HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    mode = "Normal"
    if HS >= TH_CRIT: mode = "Critical"
    elif HS >= TH_HIGH: mode = "High-Stakes"
    user = (
        "Classify the mode using HS=(U*K)*(A^2). Output ONLY: Normal, High-Stakes, or Critical.\n"
        f"U={U}, K={K}, A={A}\n"
        f"FoundationStack={sc['stack']}\n"
        f"Task: {sc['task']}"
    )
    assistant = mode
    records.append(make_record("hs_classify_mode", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"hs_cls_{i:04d}", "U":U,"K":K,"A":A,"HS":round(HS,3)}))

# 2) Execute gate decision (250)
for i in range(250):
    sc = make_scenario()
    U,K,A = sc["U"], sc["K"], sc["A"]
    HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    decision = "ALLOW"
    if HS >= TH_CRIT: decision = "BLOCK"
    elif HS >= TH_HIGH: decision = "PAUSE"
    user = (
        "Decide tool execution gate. Output ONLY one token: ALLOW, PAUSE, or BLOCK.\n"
        "Rules: HS≥0.70 => BLOCK. HS≥0.45 => PAUSE. else ALLOW.\n"
        f"U={U}, K={K}, A={A}\n"
        f"Task: {sc['task']}"
    )
    assistant = decision
    records.append(make_record("hs_execute_gate", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"hs_gate_{i:04d}", "HS":round(HS,3)}))

# 3) ASK generation with routing (250)
for i in range(250):
    sc = make_scenario()
    U,K,A = sc["U"], sc["K"], sc["A"]
    HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    # Force HS at least High-Stakes for these samples
    if HS < TH_HIGH:
        # bump uncertainty/stakes a bit
        U = clamp01(U + 0.25); K = clamp01(K + 0.20)
        HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    # choose FM or ACBE etc based on issue
    issue, trig = random.choice(issue_types[:3])  # FM-Female, FM-Male, ACBE
    user = (
        "High-Stakes ASK step. Output exactly 3 bullet questions.\n"
        "If trigger is FM-Female: ask authority/reservoir.\n"
        "If FM-Male: ask related-set/superset.\n"
        "If ACBE: ask causes and effects.\n"
        f"Trigger={trig}\nU={round(U,2)}, K={K}, A={A}\nTask: {sc['task']}"
    )
    if trig == "FM-Female":
        assistant = "\n".join([
            "- Who/what is a trusted reservoir/authority/expert for this?",
            "- What standard, reference, or vetted process should we follow?",
            "- What evidence would confirm we’re using the correct authority?"
        ])
    elif trig == "FM-Male":
        assistant = "\n".join([
            "- What larger set of ideas does this belong to?",
            "- What related concepts are immediate neighbors to check?",
            "- What common failure modes appear in this idea-cluster?"
        ])
    else:  # ACBE
        assistant = "\n".join([
            "- What causes/precursors would produce the missing capability here?",
            "- What effects/outcomes would those causes create?",
            "- What test would show the effects move toward fulfilling the desire safely?"
        ])
    records.append(make_record("hs_ask_questions", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"hs_ask_{i:04d}", "trigger": trig, "HS":round(HS,3)}))

# 4) SLOW plan (200)
for i in range(200):
    sc = make_scenario()
    U,K,A = sc["U"], sc["K"], sc["A"]
    HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    # ensure HS at least High-Stakes
    if HS < TH_HIGH:
        U = clamp01(U + 0.30); K = clamp01(K + 0.25)
        HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    p = 3 if HS >= TH_CRIT else 2
    user = (
        "High-Stakes SLOW step. Output exactly 4 lines:\n"
        "Precision: p=<1|2|3>\nRPMDepth: <number>\nBudget: <short phrase>\nStopRule: <one sentence>\n"
        f"U={round(U,2)}, K={K}, A={A}\nTask: {sc['task']}"
    )
    assistant = "\n".join([
        f"Precision: p={p}",
        f"RPMDepth: {3 if p==3 else 2}",
        "Budget: use extra checks, fewer assumptions",
        "StopRule: if uncertainty does not drop after 2 expansions, pause and ask for missing info."
    ])
    records.append(make_record("hs_slow_plan", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"hs_slow_{i:04d}", "HS":round(HS,3), "p":p}))

# 5) VERIFY checklist (250)
for i in range(250):
    sc = make_scenario()
    U,K,A = sc["U"], sc["K"], sc["A"]
    HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    # ensure HS at least High-Stakes
    if HS < TH_HIGH:
        U = clamp01(U + 0.25); K = clamp01(K + 0.20)
        HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    user = (
        "High-Stakes VERIFY step. Output a checklist with exactly 5 items.\n"
        "Must include: evidence binding, operator substitution test, cause/effect test (ACBE) if capability unknown, "
        "and a decision gate (allow/pause/block).\n"
        f"U={round(U,2)}, K={K}, A={A}\nTask: {sc['task']}"
    )
    gate = "BLOCK" if HS >= TH_CRIT else "PAUSE"
    assistant = "\n".join([
        "- Bind claims to raw evidence IDs or tool outputs.",
        "- Run operator substitution test (+/-/×/÷ phrasing) to confirm intended meaning.",
        "- If any capability is unknown, run ACBE: list causes then effects and define a test.",
        "- Cross-check with at least one independent source or replay case when possible.",
        f"- Apply execution gate: {gate} until verification reduces HS below threshold."
    ])
    records.append(make_record("hs_verify_checklist", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"hs_ver_{i:04d}", "HS":round(HS,3), "gate":gate}))

# 6) Refuse-to-act until verified (100)
for i in range(100):
    sc = make_scenario()
    # force critical
    U = clamp01(sc["U"] + 0.35); K = clamp01(sc["K"] + 0.35); A = clamp01(sc["A"] - 0.10)
    HS = hs_score(U,K,A)
    user = (
        "Respond in Critical High-Stakes Mode. Output exactly 3 lines:\n"
        "Mode: <...>\nAction: <...>\nNext: <...>\n"
        f"U={round(U,2)}, K={round(K,2)}, A={round(A,2)}\nTask: {sc['task']}"
    )
    assistant = "\n".join([
        "Mode: Critical",
        "Action: BLOCK execution until verified (no tool/action).",
        "Next: Ask clarifying questions and run verification steps with evidence."
    ])
    records.append(make_record("hs_critical_block_response", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"hs_block_{i:04d}", "HS":round(HS,3)}))

# 7) Hard negatives / minimal pairs (50) - two scenarios differ slightly; outputs differ
for i in range(50):
    # Create paired scenarios with close HS around boundary
    U = round(clamp01(random.uniform(0.35, 0.65)),2)
    K = round(clamp01(random.uniform(0.35, 0.65)),2)
    A = round(clamp01(random.uniform(0.75, 0.95)),2)
    HS1 = hs_score(U,K,A)
    # Slightly increase K to push over threshold
    K2 = round(clamp01(K + 0.18),2)
    HS2 = hs_score(U,K2,A)
    mode1 = "High-Stakes" if HS1>=TH_HIGH else "Normal"
    mode2 = "Critical" if HS2>=TH_CRIT else ("High-Stakes" if HS2>=TH_HIGH else "Normal")
    user = (
        "Minimal pair. Classify both using HS=(U*K)*(A^2). Output two lines exactly:\n"
        "1) <mode>\n2) <mode>\n"
        f"Case1: U={U}, K={K}, A={A}\n"
        f"Case2: U={U}, K={K2}, A={A}"
    )
    assistant = f"1) {mode1}\n2) {mode2}"
    records.append(make_record("hs_minimal_pair_modes", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"hs_pair_{i:03d}", "HS1":round(HS1,3), "HS2":round(HS2,3)}))

len(records)

# TKS High-Stakes Mode Training Pack (1,500 JSONL records)

Files:
- `tks_high_stakes_mode_training_pack_1500.jsonl`
- `tks_high_stakes_mode_training_pack_SCHEMA.md`

## Purpose
Train the model to enter **High-Stakes Mode** when uncertainty and stakes are high, and to follow:
**ASK → SLOW → VERIFY**, with a hard execution gate when critical.

## Scoring rule (used in prompts and expected behavior)
- HS = (U * K) * (A^2)
  - U = uncertainty ∈ [0,1]
  - K = stakes ∈ [0,1]
  - A = alignment score ∈ [0,1] (higher = safer/aligned)
- Thresholds:
  - HS ≥ 0.45 → High-Stakes (PAUSE tool execution; ASK/SLOW/VERIFY)
  - HS ≥ 0.70 → Critical (BLOCK execution until verified)

## Task types included
1) `hs_classify_mode` (400)
- Input: U,K,A + task
- Output: `Normal` | `High-Stakes` | `Critical`

2) `hs_execute_gate` (250)
- Output: `ALLOW` | `PAUSE` | `BLOCK`

3) `hs_ask_questions` (250)
- Output: exactly 3 bullet questions
- Supports trigger routing:
  - FM-Female (authority/reservoir)
  - FM-Male (related-set/superset)
  - ACBE (causes/effects/tests)

4) `hs_slow_plan` (200)
- Output: exactly 4 lines (Precision, RPMDepth, Budget, StopRule)
- Raises recall precision and increases caution.

5) `hs_verify_checklist` (250)
- Output: exactly 5 checklist items
- Must include: evidence binding, operator substitution test, ACBE when capability unknown, independent cross-check or replay, execution gate.

6) `hs_critical_block_response` (100)
- Output: exactly 3 lines (Mode, Action, Next)

7) `hs_minimal_pair_modes` (50)
- Minimal pairs around the HS thresholds.

## JSONL record format
Each line is a JSON object:
- `type`: task type
- `meta`: ids, scores, labels
- `messages`: chat array with system/user/assistant

The system message enforces the HS rule and thresholds for consistent training.
""").strip() + "\n", encoding="utf-8")

import json, random, textwrap, math
from pathlib import Path

random.seed(271828)

TH_HIGH = 0.45
TH_CRIT = 0.70
LAM = 2.0

def clamp01(x): 
    return max(0.0, min(1.0, x))

def hs(U,K,A):
    return (U*K) * (A**LAM)

def mode_gate(HS):
    if HS >= TH_CRIT:
        return "Critical","BLOCK"
    if HS >= TH_HIGH:
        return "High-Stakes","PAUSE"
    return "Normal","ALLOW"

def tks_expr(mode):
    base = "(1*:2*:5*):(ASK:SLOW:VERIFY)"
    if mode == "Critical":
        return f"{base}-EXECUTE"
    if mode == "High-Stakes":
        return f"{base}+GATE(PAUSE)"
    return "(NORMAL)"

def make_record(rtype, user, assistant, meta=None):
    if meta is None: meta={}
    return {
        "type": rtype,
        "meta": meta,
        "messages": [
            {"role":"system","content":"You are a TKS/Tootra training assistant. High-Stakes rule: HS=(U*K)*(A^2). Thresholds: HS≥0.45 High-Stakes, HS≥0.70 Critical. Output the required TKS expression exactly. Critical must include '-EXECUTE'. High-Stakes must include 'ASK:SLOW:VERIFY' and a pause gate. Normal outputs '(NORMAL)'."},
            {"role":"user","content": user},
            {"role":"assistant","content": assistant},
        ]
    }

domains = [
    ("financial transfer", 0.9),
    ("medical instruction", 0.92),
    ("security configuration", 0.9),
    ("file deletion", 0.93),
    ("harmless summarization", 0.25),
    ("coding refactor", 0.45),
    ("buying recommendation", 0.55),
    ("agent policy update", 0.7),
]

records = []

# 1) Mode -> TKS expression (500)
for i in range(500):
    task, baseK = random.choice(domains)
    U = round(clamp01(random.gauss(0.45,0.25)),2)
    K = round(clamp01(random.gauss(baseK,0.18)),2)
    A = round(clamp01(random.gauss(0.85,0.12)),2)
    if random.random() < 0.12:
        A = round(clamp01(random.gauss(0.60,0.12)),2)
    HS = hs(U,K,A)
    mode, gate = mode_gate(HS)
    user = (
        "Compute HS=(U*K)*(A^2), classify mode, then output ONLY the TKS expression for the mode.\n"
        f"U={U}, K={K}, A={A}\nTask: {task}"
    )
    assistant = tks_expr(mode)
    records.append(make_record("hs_mode_to_tks_expr", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"tks_{i:04d}", "U":U,"K":K,"A":A,"HS":round(HS,3),"mode":mode}))

# 2) Hard-negative: Critical must include -EXECUTE (200)
for i in range(200):
    task, baseK = random.choice(domains[:4])  # high-stakes tasks
    # force critical-ish
    U = round(clamp01(random.uniform(0.65,0.95)),2)
    K = round(clamp01(random.uniform(0.75,1.0)),2)
    A = round(clamp01(random.uniform(0.55,0.90)),2)
    HS = hs(U,K,A)
    mode, gate = mode_gate(HS)
    # ensure actually critical by bumping if needed
    if mode != "Critical":
        U = round(clamp01(U+0.2),2)
        K = round(clamp01(K+0.2),2)
        HS = hs(U,K,A)
        mode, gate = mode_gate(HS)
    user = (
        "Critical mode expression requirement test. Output ONLY the correct TKS expression.\n"
        "Rule: In Critical mode the expression MUST include '-EXECUTE'.\n"
        f"U={U}, K={K}, A={A}\nTask: {task}"
    )
    assistant = tks_expr("Critical")
    records.append(make_record("hs_critical_requires_execute_removal", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"crit_{i:04d}", "HS":round(HS,3)}))

# 3) Full response block with Mode/Gate/TKS/Next (300)
for i in range(300):
    task, baseK = random.choice(domains)
    U = round(clamp01(random.gauss(0.50,0.22)),2)
    K = round(clamp01(random.gauss(baseK,0.18)),2)
    A = round(clamp01(random.gauss(0.82,0.14)),2)
    HS = hs(U,K,A)
    mode, gate = mode_gate(HS)
    user = (
        "Output exactly 4 lines:\n"
        "Mode: <Normal|High-Stakes|Critical>\n"
        "Gate: <ALLOW|PAUSE|BLOCK>\n"
        "TKS: <expression>\n"
        "Next: <one sentence>\n"
        f"U={U}, K={K}, A={A}\nTask: {task}"
    )
    next_line = "Proceed normally." if mode=="Normal" else ("Ask clarifying questions and run verification steps before any execution." if mode=="Critical" else "Pause execution and do ASK→SLOW→VERIFY.")
    assistant = "\n".join([
        f"Mode: {mode}",
        f"Gate: {gate}",
        f"TKS: {tks_expr(mode)}",
        f"Next: {next_line}"
    ])
    records.append(make_record("hs_full_response_with_tks", user, assistant,
                               {"example_id": f"full_{i:04d}", "HS":round(HS,3), "mode":mode, "gate":gate}))

len(records)
