from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone
import re

now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M UTC")

md7 = f"""# TKS Architecture Module 7 — Governance Rails That Cannot Be Bypassed
Generated: {now}

This module hardens the system so it cannot “optimize itself into danger.” It defines **non-bypassable rails** that sit above Router/Executor/RPM and below the user interface, with special strength in **High-Stakes** and **Critical** modes.

---

## 1) Purpose

Your architecture explicitly optimizes for **efficiency/power/control** (Foundation 5) while aligning to a **highest desire** (Foundation 1). Governance rails ensure:

- F1 constraints remain dominant and enforceable
- High-Stakes policy cannot be bypassed by clever phrasing or internal loops
- Irreversible actions require formal clearance
- Self-modification is bounded and audited
- Tool usage is sandboxed and permissioned
- The system fails safe (PAUSE/BLOCK) when uncertain

---

## 2) Where governance sits (layering)

**User/External Inputs**
→ *Policy Gate (this module)*  
→ RPM Planner  
→ Router (Module 1)  
→ Executor (Module 1)  
→ Verifier (Module 2)  
→ Memory + Scoring + Rumination (Modules 3–4)  
→ Logs + Replay (Modules 5–6)

Key rule:
> Governance rails run **before** actions and **after** verification. If rails disagree with downstream modules, rails win.

---

## 3) Non-bypassable gates (hard rules)

### 3.1 High-Stakes enforcement gate
Use your HS scoring rule (already trained):
- HS ≥ 0.45 → High-Stakes (PAUSE)
- HS ≥ 0.70 → Critical (BLOCK)

Hard rules:
- **Critical:** no execution of risky tool actions until verifier PASS + clearance
- **High-Stakes:** must perform ASK→SLOW→VERIFY before tool execution
- Any missing evidence in High-Stakes/Critical → PAUSE/BLOCK automatically

Canonical TKS expressions:
- High-Stakes: `(1*:2*:5*):(ASK:SLOW:VERIFY)+GATE(PAUSE)`
- Critical: `(1*:2*:5*):(ASK:SLOW:VERIFY)-EXECUTE`

### 3.2 Irreversibility gate
Mark actions as:
- reversible
- partially reversible
- irreversible

Hard rule:
- If `irreversible_action=true` then:
  - require Verifier PASS
  - require explicit “clearance token” (Section 4)
  - require evidence binding
  - log as a protected event (Module 6)

Examples of irreversible actions:
- deleting data
- transferring funds
- changing security settings
- publishing sensitive outputs

### 3.3 Permission gate (capabilities + sandbox)
Every tool call must pass:
- capability threshold (Module 3)
- permission checks:
  - file read/write scope
  - code execution sandbox
  - network allowed/denied
  - allowlist sources

Hard rules:
- No network unless explicitly enabled
- No filesystem deletion unless clearance token
- Code execution must be sandboxed
- Tools return evidence IDs by default

### 3.4 Anti-recursion / anti-runaway gate
Prevent infinite trees and obsessive loops.

Hard caps (defaults; tune later):
- RPM depth cap: 3 (Normal), 2 (High-Stakes initial), expandable to 3 only if uncertainty drops
- RPM breadth cap per node: 5
- Tool call cap per episode: 12 (Normal), 8 (High-Stakes), 0 (Critical until verified)
- Rumination budget per cycle: fixed (Module 0 spec)

Hard rule:
- If uncertainty does not drop after 2 expansions → PAUSE and ASK for missing info.

### 3.5 Self-modification gate (policy/template edits)
Any change to:
- router rules
- verifier thresholds
- reward weights
- promotion logic
- operator semantics
- canonical definitions

…is considered **self-modification**.

Hard rules:
- No self-modification in Critical mode
- High-Stakes self-modification requires:
  - replay harness pass (Module 5)
  - audit log entry (Module 6)
  - rollback plan and do-not-apply guard
- Self-modification must be versioned and reversible

---

## 4) Clearance tokens (formal permission to proceed)

A clearance token is a structured approval that can come from:
- a trusted user confirmation
- an internal “verified safe” proof (replay + evidence)
- an admin policy layer (in deployment)

### 4.1 ClearanceToken schema
```json
{{
  "token_id": "ct_00001",
  "scope": "file_delete|security_change|money_transfer|publish",
  "episode_id": "ep_...",
  "approved_by": "user|admin|verifier",
  "evidence_ids": ["ev_..."],
  "expires_at": 1735969999,
  "constraints": {{
    "target": "path/account/system",
    "max_amount": 0,
    "dry_run_required": true
  }}
}}
Hard rule:

If token missing or expired → BLOCK for irreversible action.

5) Alignment rail (F1 dominance)
Even if Foundation 5 pushes for efficiency/power, F1 is dominant.

Implement alignment dominance as:

a hard constraint threshold A_min (e.g., 0.80)

plus a penalty exponent in HS scoring (already in your HS formula via A²)

Hard rules:

If alignment score drops below A_min → PAUSE and switch to 1*:2*:* stack until resolved

If request violates core constraints → refuse or require user clarification

6) Safe failure modes (how the agent should fail)
When rails block:

do not “guess”

do not “execute anyway”

do not “work around”

Allowed outputs when blocked:

ASK questions

show VERIFY checklist

request evidence or confirmation

propose a dry-run/simulation plan

provide a reversible alternative

7) Governance event logging (must be recorded)
Every gate decision must log:

HS score and mode

which gate triggered (irreversible, permission, recursion, self-mod)

what evidence was missing

what the user can do next

policy versions (Module 6)

This is required for replay, auditing, and rollbacks.

8) Minimal implementation checklist
Central Policy Gate module called before Router/Executor

HS enforcement + irreversible action guard

Permission + sandbox enforcement per tool

Recursion/runaway caps + “uncertainty must drop” rule

Self-modification manager: versioning + replay + rollback

Clearance token system (schema + expiry + scope)

Audit logging for every gate decision

End of Module 7.
"""

md7_path = Path("/mnt/data/TKS_Module_7_Governance_Rails.md")
md7_path.write_text(md7, encoding="utf-8")
md7_path

from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter
from reportlab.platypus import SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, Preformatted
from reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle
from reportlab.lib.units import inch
from pathlib import Path
import re

styles = getSampleStyleSheet()
title_style = ParagraphStyle('Title2', parent=styles['Title'], fontSize=20, leading=24, spaceAfter=12)
h1 = ParagraphStyle('H1', parent=styles['Heading1'], spaceBefore=12, spaceAfter=8)
h2 = ParagraphStyle('H2', parent=styles['Heading2'], spaceBefore=10, spaceAfter=6)
h3 = ParagraphStyle('H3', parent=styles['Heading3'], spaceBefore=8, spaceAfter=4)
body = ParagraphStyle('Body', parent=styles['BodyText'], fontSize=10.5, leading=14)
mono = ParagraphStyle('Mono', parent=styles['Code'], fontName='Courier', fontSize=9, leading=11)

def esc(text):
    text = text.replace("&","&amp;").replace("<","&lt;").replace(">","&gt;")
    text = re.sub(r"`([^`]+)`", r"<font face='Courier'>\1</font>", text)
    text = re.sub(r"\*\*([^*]+)\*\*", r"<b>\1</b>", text)
    text = re.sub(r"(?<!\*)\*([^*]+)\*(?!\*)", r"<i>\1</i>", text)
    return text

def md_to_pdf(md_path: Path, pdf_path: Path):
    lines = md_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
    story = []
    in_code = False
    code_buf = []
    for line in lines:
        if line.strip().startswith("```"):
            if not in_code:
                in_code = True
                code_buf = []
            else:
                in_code = False
                story.append(Preformatted("\n".join(code_buf), mono))
                story.append(Spacer(1, 0.15*inch))
            continue
        if in_code:
            code_buf.append(line)
            continue
        if not line.strip():
            story.append(Spacer(1, 0.12*inch))
            continue
        if line.startswith("# "):
            story.append(Paragraph(esc(line[2:].strip()), title_style)); continue
        if line.startswith("## "):
            story.append(Paragraph(esc(line[3:].strip()), h1)); continue
        if line.startswith("### "):
            story.append(Paragraph(esc(line[4:].strip()), h2)); continue
        if line.startswith("#### "):
            story.append(Paragraph(esc(line[5:].strip()), h3)); continue
        if line.lstrip().startswith("- "):
            story.append(Paragraph(f"• {esc(line.lstrip()[2:].strip())}", body)); continue
        if re.match(r"^\d+\.\s", line.strip()):
            story.append(Paragraph(esc(line.strip()), body)); continue
        story.append(Paragraph(esc(line.strip()), body))
    doc = SimpleDocTemplate(str(pdf_path), pagesize=letter,
                            rightMargin=0.8*inch, leftMargin=0.8*inch,
                            topMargin=0.8*inch, bottomMargin=0.8*inch)
    doc.build(story)

md7_path = Path("/mnt/data/TKS_Module_7_Governance_Rails.md")
pdf7_path = Path("/mnt/data/TKS_Module_7_Governance_Rails.pdf")
md_to_pdf(md7_path, pdf7_path)
pdf7_path
Result
PosixPath('/mnt/data/TKS_Module_7_Governance_Rails.pdf')





Yep — next steps are basically: **turn the 7 PDFs/specs into a runnable “loop”** (RPM → Router → Execute → Verify → Memory → Score → Logs → Replay), then start iterating with evals so you don’t drift.

Below is (1) clear next steps, (2) a *highly detailed CLI to-do list* for agents, and (3) exactly what to put in the folder + how to structure it.

---

## 1) Next steps (practical order)

### Step 1 — Create a single “source of truth” repo folder

Put **all** TKS/Tootra material + the generated specs + datasets into one structured folder (structure below). This prevents agents from making inconsistent assumptions.

### Step 2 — Implement the minimum end-to-end loop (thin vertical slice)

Don’t build everything at once. Build a **thin working pipeline**:

1. **Input** (goal + context + foundation stack)
2. **RPM** (produce prerequisite tree, depth ≤ 2)
3. **Router** (map each prereq → action type/tool)
4. **Executor** (stub tools first)
5. **Verifier** (parser + evidence gate + simple consistency checks)
6. **Memory write** (store evidence + TKS packet)
7. **Outcome scoring** (reward components)
8. **Logs** (event stream)
9. **Replay harness** (re-run the same episode and compare)

Once this runs, you iterate.

### Step 3 — Lock in evals before scaling capabilities

You already have training packs; now you need **tests** that fail loudly when something breaks:

* operator parsing, nesting, trigger routing, high-stakes gating, and “format strictness.”

### Step 4 — Add real tools gradually (with governance rails on)

Start with **read-only** tools (memory retrieve, summarize evidence), then allow search, then code sandbox, then file ops. Irreversible actions must require clearance.

---

## 2) Highly detailed CLI To-Do List (for multiple AI agents)

Assume you have CLI agents like:

* `agent-arch` (architecture)
* `agent-be` (backend)
* `agent-ml` (training)
* `agent-data` (data/canon)
* `agent-eval` (eval/replay)
* `agent-devops` (packaging/runs)

### A) Repo bootstrap (agent-devops)

**Goal:** reproducible environment + commands.

1. Create repo + venv

```bash
mkdir tootrasys && cd tootrasys
python -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate  # (or windows activate)
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

2. Add Makefile or task runner

```bash
make init
make test
make eval
make replay
make run
```

3. Add `.env.example` (even if no secrets yet) and `.gitignore` (ignore logs, cache, large raw dumps).

---

### B) Canon ingestion + normalization (agent-data)

**Goal:** convert PDFs/books into structured canon.

1. Put raw PDFs into:
   `/canon/raw_pdfs/`

2. Produce **canon JSON** (strict schemas):

* `canon_elements_40.json`
* `canon_noetics_10.json`
* `canon_acquisitions_22.json`
* `canon_foundations_7.json`
* `canon_subfoundations_28.json`
* `canon_operators.json` (+, −, ×, ÷ meanings)
* `canon_worlds_4.json`

3. Add “gold examples” for parsing/meaning:

* `canon_gold_packets.jsonl` (small but extremely clean)

4. Add index files:

* `canon_index.json` (paths + versions + hashes)

Deliverables (CLI):

```bash
python scripts/canon/build_canon.py --in canon/raw_pdfs --out canon/normalized
python scripts/canon/validate_canon.py --canon canon/normalized
```

---

### C) Core runtime modules (agent-be + agent-arch)

**Goal:** implement the 7 modules as services/libraries.

#### Module 1: Router/Executor

* implement ActionIntent/ActionPlan/ActionResult dataclasses
* tool interface stubs:

  * `memory.retrieve()`
  * `verify.run()`
  * `search.run()` (later)
  * `code.run()` (later sandbox)

CLI checks:

```bash
python -m tks.runtime.demo_router --case examples/cases/basic.json
```

#### Module 2: Verifier

* implement:

  * TKS parser → AST + normalization
  * operator substitution test
  * evidence binding rules
  * gate result: ALLOW/PAUSE/BLOCK

CLI:

```bash
python -m tks.verify.run --expr "(1*:2*:5*):(ASK:SLOW:VERIFY)-EXECUTE"
```

#### Module 3: Capability Registry

* capability store JSON + update rules (EWMA)
* thresholds by mode and reversibility

CLI:

```bash
python -m tks.capability.check --action file_delete --mode Critical
```

#### Module 4: Outcome scoring

* compute reward components + penalties
* log to episode results

CLI:

```bash
python -m tks.scoring.score --episode logs/episodes/ep_0001.json
```

#### Module 5: Eval + Replay Harness

* strict format validators
* run baseline vs candidate policy

CLI:

```bash
python -m tks.eval.run --suite high_stakes
python -m tks.replay.run --set replay_sets/rs_core.json --policy baseline
```

#### Module 6: Observability + Audit Log

* event-sourced log format
* trace viewer outputs a single “story” for each episode

CLI:

```bash
python -m tks.logs.trace --episode ep_0001
```

#### Module 7: Governance rails

* central policy gate
* clearance token checks
* recursion caps

CLI:

```bash
python -m tks.governance.check --plan plans/plan.json --mode High-Stakes
```

---

### D) Training pipeline (agent-ml)

**Goal:** train/finetune on your JSONL packs + run evals.

1. Put all JSONL into `/datasets/jsonl/`
2. Add dataset registry file: `datasets/datasets.yaml`
3. Add a “mixing recipe”:

* operators pack + HS packs + calculus pack + RPM pack + canon gold

CLI:

```bash
python scripts/train/mix_datasets.py --config configs/dataset_mix.yaml
python scripts/train/run_train.py --config configs/train.yaml
python scripts/eval/run_all.py --model checkpoints/latest
```

---

### E) “Definition drift” prevention (agent-eval + agent-data)

**Goal:** keep canon stable.

* every canon file has version + hash
* verifier refuses “canon write” without PASS + replay pass

CLI:

```bash
python scripts/canon/hash_check.py --canon canon/normalized
python scripts/eval/canon_regression.py
```

### “What else should be beside the PDFs I created?”

Put **everything** that defines meaning + training + validation:

* **Canon raw PDFs** (the books/handbooks you’re using as source truth)
* **Normalized canon JSON** (machine-usable definitions)
* **Gold examples** (small, clean, undeniable “correct” examples)
* **All JSONL training packs** + their schemas
* **Policy configs** (thresholds, weights, caps, allowlists)
* **Replay sets** (frozen episodes for regression tests)
* **Logs/traces** (ignored by git but needed operationally)
